# Status Log

Append-only journal of working sessions. Newest entries on top. Each entry should answer three questions in 5–15 lines: **what was done**, **what's next**, **gotchas / open questions**.

Write a fresh entry at the end of every session, before stopping. Do not edit older entries — if something turned out wrong, note it in the next entry.

Rotation: keep roughly the last 10 sessions here; move older entries to `docs/archive/` every 20–30 sessions. V1's log lives in `docs/archive/STATUS-sessions-01-88.md` and `STATUS-sessions-89-98.md`.

---

## Session 162 (V2 Session 64) — 2026-08-22

**Done — Phase 152e, which closes M-P3: one feature and four negatives.** Branch `phase-152e-angle-chase`, five commits, merged to `main` (deployed). Suite **3107** green (3083 + 24), analyze clean, browser gate 13 green.

- **The feature: an AR step now reads as the angle chase it is** (`angle_chase.dart`). 152d shipped `angle_arithmetic from [1], [2], [4], [6], [8]` into the panel — it names what a step used and explains nothing, which is the complaint PLAN refused Wu and Gröbner over, arriving through the front door. A step *is* a sum of relations, so the sum is what is shown: one line per equation in θ language, indented under the step, in `Proof.render()` and in the panel alike. On `perp-true-unproved.rgl`: `θ(AB) = θ(CB) + π/2 [1]` / `θ(CB) = θ(FC) [2]` / … / `⟹ θ(EA) = θ(FC) + π/2`.
- **Two design points worth keeping.** A chase line is an **equation, not a premise fact** — the TODO's own warning, honoured: a `coll` contributes two equations and a certificate may weight them differently, so "the coefficient of step [4]" is not a number. And the chase is **re-derived at render time, never stored** — the same choice `derivation_check.dart` makes, so nothing is added to a `Derivation` and the save format does not move. Lines read out in *citation* order so the eye never jumps back; the `θ(AB)`/`θ(A,B)` separator is decided once per chase, because `θ(auxd)` reads as a point that does not exist. Mutation-checked both ways.
- **Four measurements, each taken before building the thing it was about — and every one came back zero.** (a) The **incidence bridge**, measured in its eager upper-bound form: 0 new `para`/`perp` on all five fixtures. (b) **Deleting `coll_transitive`/`perp_coll`** with AR running: loses facts on three of five (`apatitos-topos` 30 → 28, `perp-true-unproved` 50 → 39, `provoleas2` 81 → 57), and the bridge recovers 12 of `provoleas2`'s 24 and *none* of `perp-true-unproved`'s 11. (c) **DD asking AR for `eqangle`**: AR entails 129/89/33/272/**2527** eqangles beyond the database — none refused by the filter — and neither the safe *guard* form (`sas_simtri`, `aa_simtri`: 0 new `simtri`) nor the unsafe *generator* form (the two converses: 0 new `cyclic`, 0 new `cong`) derives a single new fact. (d) The **length system**, simulated as the segment union-find it reduces to when fed only `cong`: 0 new `cong`, and the corpus holds one `eqratio` fact in total.
- **What the four add up to, which no single box says.** The angle half of AR pays and pays well (41 → 93 on the JGEX document, nothing lost anywhere). Everything *else* M-P3 listed measures to zero **on this corpus**. Four independent measurements landing on zero is not four coincidences: **the corpus is the limiting factor, not the algebra.** Recorded in PLAN §M-P3 so the next session does not re-derive it.
- **Two corrections to the boxes themselves.** The `eqangle`-ask box said the ask is why `apatitos-topos.rgl` gains nothing; it is not — AR entails 89 more eqangles there, and the gap is that `eqangle`'s only consumers (`eqangle_transitive`, the two converses, the two `simtri` rules) find no new instance. And the rule-deletion box assumed `perp_coll` being derivable as arithmetic made it deletable; the `coll` half of the losses is unrecoverable **in principle**, because collinearity is not a statement about directions.

**Next.** **Phase 153 — auxiliary construction** (JGEX's A2), which the four negatives point at directly: the machinery is ahead of the corpus, and A2 is what makes a document answerable that is not answerable now. Worth pairing with **new fixtures** whose theorems are inscribed-angle and ratio chases — that is what would make the bridge, the ask and the length system pay, and until one exists none of them can be justified. Also still open: the id-based fact transfer for `Isolate.run`; M-P4's point-merge *Show why*; Phase 139's open box; Android/iOS smokes; the pure route for deflation; the direct/reflected `simtri` split.

**Browser.** Merged and deployed; all workflows green. On the live build a Varignon proof renders its seven steps cleanly with **no chase lines on any DD step** — the property the panel change could have broken. Varignon derives no angle steps at all, so seeing a real chase live needs `perp-true-unproved.rgl` loaded through the file picker, which browser automation cannot drive; the chase's glyphs (`θ`, `π`, `⟹`, three different Unicode blocks, reaching the page as string literals through the web backend) are pinned in `test/web/` instead — gate **13**.

**Gotchas.** A **carrier publisher** is the named prerequisite for ever deleting `coll_transitive` — `CarrierIndex` knows those `coll` facts and nothing puts them in the database; 151a's sweep already pins that the closure finds every `coll` the rule derives. `para_transitive`, `perp_perp_para`, `para_perp_perp` and `eqangle_transitive` were **not** measured separately and should be, one at a time — Phase 151b's lesson is that a batch deletion makes the cause unreadable. And the panel's chase was verified in `testWidgets`, not yet in a browser; the deployed Varignon figure derives no angle steps at all, so seeing it live needs the JGEX document loaded.

## Session 161 (V2 Session 63) — 2026-08-22

**Done — Phase 152 / M-P3 end to end: the arithmetic decided, the algebra built, the vocabulary mapped, the two engines interleaved, and the whole thing wired to the panel.** Continues `phase-151-incidence-closure`; six more commits. Suite **3083** green, analyze clean, browser gate green (6), `flutter build web --wasm --release` compiles.

- **The decision came before the code, and it changed the data structure.** M-P3's one-line description said closure is Gaussian elimination. It is right about the variables and wrong about the elimination. Each θ is defined only mod π, so a linear form is a statement *only* when its coefficients are integers — and while every equation the vocabulary produces starts at ±1, elimination does not keep them there. Two ordinary `eqangle`s (`∠(l₁,l₂) = ∠(l₃,l₄)` and `∠(l₁,l₂) = ∠(l₄,l₃)`) sum to `2θ₂ − 2θ₁ ≡ 0`; over ℚ the pivot divides by two and concludes **`para`**, when the content is `2∠(l₁,l₂) ≡ 0 (mod π)` — parallel **or** perpendicular, and a real figure can be either. So AR is a **ℤ-module**: echelon form by gcd combination, never dividing a row by its leading coefficient, entailment as ℤ-span membership. A `2θ` row is kept for further combination and publishes nothing; reading the branch off the diagram would be diagram-dependent, which is *not* what the filter does elsewhere (it screens conclusions already entailed). PLAN §"AR is a ℤ-module, not a ℚ-vector space".
- **152a — `rational.dart` + `angle_closure.dart`.** `BigInt`-backed rationals, because `int` is 64-bit on the VM and under dart2wasm and a double-backed 53-bit integer under dart2js, and exactness must not depend on the target. Provenance from day one on the `FactDatabase` precedent: every row is an integer combination of inputs, `entails` answers with that certificate, `recombine` multiplies it back. **Mutation-checked**: normalizing an installed row by its coefficient gcd — what a rational pivot amounts to — fails exactly the two tests that encode the decision.
- **152b — `angle_translation.dart`, and two corrections to the plan.** A variable is a point **pair**, not a carrier: two pairs on one carrier do have equal θ, but equal θ is *parallel*, not *identical*, and a carrier moves when carriers merge (Phase 151c's mutable key, again). And **incidence does not reduce to the algebra** — `coll(a,b,c)` with `coll(a,b,d)` leaves the closure knowing `θ_ab = θ_ac = θ_bc = θ_ad = θ_bd` and *nothing* about `θ_cd`, because the pair `cd` names no variable; that `c` and `d` are each on line `ab` is incidence, and directions cannot express it. `CarrierIndex` is what knows it, and its job here is sharper than indexing: it tells a parallel from a line and itself. Rules that fall out as arithmetic with no rule: `perp_perp_para`, `para_perp_perp`, `para_transitive`, `eqangle_transitive`, Chasles — and **`perp_coll`**, the rule Phase 150 added and Phase 151 could not delete.
- **152c — `prover.dart`, the facade.** A pass is DD steps → AR reads → AR publishes → DD pivots the publication; the run ends when DD is quiescent **and** the last pass published nothing. A loop, not a pipeline. An AR step is **verified by re-derivation, not re-multiplication**: there is no rule to re-match, so `derivation_check.dart` builds a closure over the recorded premises alone and asks. Checking the stored certificate would be checking the engine's arithmetic against itself — the same choice the file already makes in restating the matcher.
- **152d — the provider switched to `Prover`.** M-P3 is now what the panel shows.
- **The numbers, cap 30 000**: `perp-true-unproved.rgl` **41 → 93 facts**, still quiescent, 202 → 312 ms, 21 angle steps; `locus3.json` 15 → 24, quiescent, no slower; `provoleas2.json` 75 → 81 at the cap; `apatitos-topos.rgl` and `no-locus.rgl` unchanged and no slower. **Nothing lost on any fixture**, and every proof of every one of the 31 angle steps verifies — which by `Proof.of`'s walk covers the DD steps underneath.

**Next.** **152e**, opened: DD *asking* AR for `eqangle` instead of AR publishing it (why `apatitos-topos` — 13 `eqangle`, 1 `perp` — gains nothing today); the query-time incidence bridge; what an AR step reads as; the **length system** (`cong`/`eqratio` over log-lengths, genuinely ℚ, and `Rational` is already what it needs); and the rule deletions **as a measurement**, Phase 151b being the standing warning. Then Phase 153 (auxiliary construction). Carried: the id-based fact transfer for `Isolate.run`; M-P4's point-merge *Show why*; Phase 139's open box; the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation; the direct/reflected `simtri` split.

**Gotchas.**

- **The widget gate caught two bugs a green unit suite said nothing about, and both were mine.** `Prover.runChunked` yielded *after* spending its last budget — a yield is a timer, and inside `testWidgets`' fake async a caller that awaits without pumping waits on a timer nobody fires, so the panel test hung for minutes instead of failing. And `maxApplications` caps *this call*, not the engine's lifetime; comparing the lifetime count made `proveMore` a no-op, so **Keep going** kept nothing going. `ProverEngine.runChunked` had both shapes right and I did not read it closely enough before writing a peer. Pinned now with `fakeAsync` + `flushMicrotasks`.
- **An AR step is still a black box in a printed proof.** The DD steps read well; `angle_arithmetic from [1], [2], [4], [6], [8]` names its premises and explains nothing. **Per-fact multiples are not the fix** — a `coll` contributes two equations and a certificate may use them differently, so there is no well-defined coefficient per cited *fact*. The angle chase itself is what would read, one line per equation, and it needs the equations at render time.
- **The budget is DD applications only, and that has a cost on a document already at the cap**: `provoleas2` gets one AR look instead of several. `perp-true-unproved` goes 3 906 → 16 034 applications because AR's conclusions are pivots — it still reaches quiescence, but the exchange is not free.
- **`fake_async` is now a declared dev dependency.** It was already transitively present through `flutter_test`; the analyzer's `depend_on_referenced_packages` is right that a direct import must be declared.
- Session 159's UI had still not been looked at in a browser when this entry was written; the merge and browser check are this session's last step.

## Session 160 (V2 Session 62) — 2026-08-22

**Done — Phase 151 / M-P2d, which shipped a data structure and three measurements, two of which say not to build the thing the phase was for.** On `phase-151-incidence-closure`, **unmerged**, five commits. Suite **3008** green (2984 + 24), analyze clean.

- **Phase 151a — the carrier index** (`lib/domain/prover/carriers.dart`): lines and circles as objects carrying their point sets, merged by union-find over the tuples that *name* them — pairs for a line, triples for a circle. **The arity is the soundness argument**: two distinct points determine a line, three determine a circle, so a merge on a shared name is the statement "these are the same line", inheriting exactly the assumption `coll_transitive` already makes and adding no new surface. The invariant that makes lookup a map read is that every arity-subset of a class's point set maps to that class, re-keyed after each merge — which is how a merge nothing *generated* gets found (`coll(a,e,f)` meeting line `{a,b,c,d}` at the pair `ad`). `lineThrough` is total: an unmentioned pair answers its own two-point carrier, which is the truth about it. Incremental, and pinned to land where a rebuild lands in any arrival order. A full rebuild over `provoleas2`'s 75 facts is **41 µs**.
- **Phase 151b — `lineSlots`, and the matcher measured and rejected.** The table says which argument pairs name a *line*: `para`/`perp` both, `eqangle` all four (mod π), `cong`/`eqratio` **none** — a pair there names a segment, and a segment has a length its carrier does not. Pinned against `Predicate.holdsOn` over a seeded sweep and *driven off the table* rather than restating it; mutation-checked, listing `cong`'s pairs fails on a numeric counterexample. Then the consumer: resolving a premise's line slots through the closure, flagged, rule table unchanged so the closure could only add. **It is a regression on every fixture.** `locus3` goes from 162 applications and quiescent, 15 facts, to **4 000 000 applications still running**, 35 s, 29 facts; `apatitos-topos` 632 apps / 1.3 s → past 30 000 / 11.7 s. And the rules are *not* subsumed: dropping `coll_transitive` / `perp_coll` / `cyclic_fifth_point` on top **loses** 16 facts on `perp-true-unproved.rgl`, including the `perp` the JGEX proof turns on, and 32 on `provoleas2`. Reverted, on Phase 150's `para_coll` precedent.
- **The mechanism, which is the reusable half.** Identity matching **prunes**: a premise bound to the wrong point fails at once and its join subtree is never walked. Carrier matching removes that pruning while the conclusion side still emits point tuples, so every newly-successful join yields another *spelling*, which is stored, which pivots, which joins more loosely still. On the premise side alone the closure is a fact multiplier. **"Cheaper to match" is not "fewer matches"** — and 151a's own note that 41 µs made resolution "affordable per join" was reading the wrong constraint, corrected in place rather than left standing.
- **Phase 151c — opened as the fact-key redesign, closed unbuilt.** Two measurements bounded it and a third made it moot, all taken off a baseline run with no engine change. *Upside*: `provoleas2`'s 53 line-shaped facts are **8 distinct statements** (75 facts → 30 keys), `perp-true-unproved` 41 → 28, and the three fixtures that already converge collapse by **nothing**. *Cost*: **10 of the 26 rules** have a variable in both a line role and a point-or-segment role — `perp_bisector`'s `cong(o,a,o,b) & cong(p,a,p,b) => perp(o,p,a,b)` binds `a`, `b` as segment ends and then names them as a line — so a plain reduced key leaves a third of the table with nothing to bind. *And then*: split the line-shaped facts by kind and they are `para` + `perp` + `eqangle`, which is **every kind AR absorbs**. Not a coincidence — `lineSlots` is non-empty for exactly the predicates whose arguments name lines, and a direction is what θ is a variable over. After M-P3 those three are not facts and `lineSlots` is empty for everything that remains. A witness-set database would deduplicate facts M-P3 deletes.
- **PLAN was amended before each conclusion, not after**, since both corrected sentences are PLAN's own: the incidence closure "deletes rules, deletes the spelling search, and makes every later rule cheaper", and the ordering that put M-P2d ahead of M-P3.

**Next.** **Phase 152 / M-P3 (AR)** — now M-P2d's consumer rather than its successor: θ is indexed per *carrier*, so `carriers.dart` is what it is built on. Its checklist carries what the DD fact set looks like afterwards (`coll`, `cyclic`, `cong`, `midp`, `eqratio`, `simtri`, `contri` — every one `lineSlots`-empty) and says that `coll_transitive`, `perp_coll` and `questions.dart`'s all-spellings search are re-examined *there*. Then Phase 153 (auxiliary construction). **`phase-151-incidence-closure` is unmerged** — merging is a deploy, so check `gh run list` after. Carried: the id-based fact transfer that would let `Isolate.run` happen; M-P4's leftover point-merge *Show why*; Phase 139's open box; the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation; the direct/reflected `simtri` split.

**Gotchas.**

- **A cheap structure is not a cheap consumer, and 151a's measurement invited exactly that confusion.** 41 µs to build the closure is true and irrelevant: the binding constraint was never the closure's cost, it was the join's *pruning*. Measure the thing the change is going to alter, not the thing it adds.
- **The split is what made the failure readable.** Had the matcher moved and three rules been deleted in one commit, a fact that stopped being derived would have had two possible causes. Every one of this session's conclusions came from changing one variable against a fixed baseline.
- **`provoleas2.json` earns its keep as the pathological fixture and `locus3` as the healthy one.** The matcher change looked survivable on the blowup document (75 → 77 facts) and was catastrophic on the small one that converges. A fixture set that only contains hard documents would have shipped this.
- **Nothing in `lib/` reads `carriers.dart` yet.** It is tested, analyzed and in the tree with no consumer until Phase 152 — deliberate, but it means the wasm smoke does not exercise it and a change to it breaks nothing but its own tests.
- **`cyclic_fifth_point` fires on no fixture**, so the circle half of the closure has no measured consumer at all. Recorded as `para_coll`'s status was, not claimed as a saving.
- None of Session 159's UI has been looked at in a browser — carried unchanged, and nothing this session touched UI.

## Session 159 (V2 Session 61) — 2026-08-21

**Done — asking the prover a question (M-P4d), two UI corrections, and then a user's JGEX screenshot that overturned two of my conclusions.** Phases 148–150 on `phase-148-ask-a-question`, branched off Session 158's `phase-145-mp4-proof-panel`. Suite **2984** green, analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green (6), wasm build compiles.

- **Phase 148 / M-P4d — the question, and the three answers.** DD is already not goal-directed (chain forward, then check membership), so `proofOf` was always the second half and only the asking was missing. `questions.dart` turns a selection into what it can phrase — two line-shaped things give `perp`/`para`, plus `cong` when both bound a length; three points give `coll` and the three `midp` readings; four give `cyclic` and the three pairings. Every selected object must be consumed or there is no question. **The verdict is three-way and the middle one is the point**: *refuted* (the filter, with no run at all), *unproved* (a finished run could not get there), *proved* — plus *undecided* for an exhausted budget, which has shown nothing about reachability. Collapsing refuted and unproved would tell a user their correct theorem was wrong.
- **`runChunked` gained `stopWhen`, and asking is cheaper than deriving.** Measured on `provoleas2.json`, which never reaches quiescence: 1 000 applications and 17.7 ms to answer a question, against 30 000 and 776.6 ms that answers nothing. That is the argument for doing this before the isolate work.
- **Phase 149 — the panels open when the user opens them.** The proof button moved beside the geometry menu; **Style & properties stopped opening itself**, which it had been doing on every selection — meaning a tap on the canvas resized the canvas under the pointer. Both are now app-bar toggles like the object tree, the button is always shown (a toolbar whose buttons come and go cannot be aimed at), and the empty panel says what to do instead of collapsing. Follow-up on request: the style button now sits between hide/delete and undo.
- **Phase 150 — the three rules a JGEX trace found, and two claims of mine corrected.** The user proved `perp(C,D,D,F)` on the very fixture Phase 148 shipped as the *unproved* rig. JGEX's dialog said how: **it constructed an auxiliary point** — the midpoint of our BC. Translating its trace step by step found `coll_transitive`, `perp_coll` and `orthocentre` missing. With the auxiliary point and those three, our own prover reaches it in 17 steps and `verify()` comes back empty. **Both halves are necessary** and the regression pins both directions.
- **`para_coll` was written, rigged, measured and deleted** — the exact analogue for parallels derives nothing new on any fixture. One line the day a document needs it; until then it pays its cost on every pivot for nothing.
- **PLAN §"Two closures the rule table is standing in for" is the session's real output.** Behind those three rules are two structural gaps. **Incidence** — a line has one identity and the vocabulary gives it many names, so `coll_transitive`/`perp_coll` and `questions.dart`'s all-spellings search are all patches for a missing union-find over carriers (now Phase 151, ahead of M-P3). And **angles are an algebra**: JGEX's first six "full angle rules" are the definition of a structure and the other twenty-three are identities in it, hard-coded as patterns. **M-P3 is rewritten from "a second rule engine" to AR** — θ per line mod π, angle facts as linear equations, closure by elimination (Phase 152). The cost argument decides it and is measurable: orbit sizes are `midp` 2, `coll` 6, `para`/`perp`/`cong` 8, **`eqangle`/`eqratio` 128**, so adding angle *rules* feeds the cost centre while moving angles into a linear system removes it.

**Next.** Phases 151 → 152 → 153 in that order (incidence closure, AR, auxiliary construction), all open in TODO with their scope written. Carried: the id-based fact transfer that would let `Isolate.run` happen; M-P4's leftover point-merge *Show why*; Phase 139's open box; the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation; the direct/reflected `simtri` split.

**Gotchas.**

- **I over-generalized twice this session, from the same experiment.** Seeding the intermediate *facts* of a proof and watching them fail to close it says the rule set cannot use them; it says nothing about what a different *construction* would produce. Both "no auxiliary construction could help" and "this needs M-P3's angle arithmetic" were wrong, and the second was wrong twice over — the theorem needs neither. The narrow claim survives: with the core as it was and the objects as they were, it was unreachable. When the question is "could more objects help", the experiment is to add objects.
- **`perp-true-unproved.rgl` is still the *unproved* rig, but only because its auxiliary point is absent.** Phase 153 will end that, and Phase 148's three-verdict tests will need a genuinely unreachable statement. Pinned explicitly now rather than incidentally.
- **A rule's cost is set by its premise *kinds*, not by the rule count.** Phase 150's three cost nothing measurable because their premises are `coll` and `perp`. Read the orbit table before adding anything with an `eqangle` premise.
- **`Proof.verify()` still checks instantiation, never the table.** Every rule added is one more numeric rig owed. Seventy-two rules would be seventy-two rigs — a further reason to prefer closures over rules.
- Phase 148's "any spelling counts" test changed character in Phase 150: the spelling it relied on being unreachable became derivable, so it moved to a budget too small to reach it. Expect the same again in Phase 151, which removes multi-spelling questions entirely.
- None of this session's UI has been looked at in a browser.

## Session 158 (V2 Session 60) — 2026-08-21

**Done — M-P4 end to end, in three phases: the boundary, the panel, and the step pointed at on the figure.** On `phase-145-mp4-proof-panel`, **unmerged**, four commits. Suite **2950** green (2913 + 37), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green (6), `flutter build web --wasm --release` compiles.

- **Phase 145 / M-P4a — the provider** (`lib/application/providers/prover_provider.dart`): a sealed `ProverState` over `hypotheses` → `DiagramFilter.probe` → `seedHypotheses` → `ProverEngine.runChunked`. On demand, never on every edit — PLAN §M-P4's own division of labour — so `prove()` is a method and the notifier deliberately does **not** watch the construction (a drag notifies per frame). A run records the revision it read and staleness is the consumer's comparison. A non-Euclidean document is a `ProverRefused` *state*: a CK document is a perfectly good document about which the Euclidean vocabulary has nothing to say.
- **The cap turned out not to be theoretical, and that is the phase's real finding.** `test/fixtures/provoleas2.json` — 27 objects, 23 hypotheses — is **still deriving after 200 000 rule applications** (13.0 s on the VM). That is the `eqangle` join blowup Session 156 named as structural and had not yet seen materialize; a real user document is what materializes it. So `runChunked` took a per-call `maxApplications`, exhaustion became a state rather than a failure, and `proveMore()` resumes the same engine. Budget 30 000 ≈ 2 s in the regime where it binds.
- **Two things settled by measurement rather than by design.** `Isolate.run` **clears PLAN's own rule by orders of magnitude** (a real fixpoint is 10 ms to 13+ s against a 0.05–0.09 ms round trip), and chunking — which keeps frames alive — does not make a 13-second answer arrive sooner; what blocks it is the id-based fact transfer that keying facts by point identity forces, and that is its own phase. And **an application's cost is not flat on the real rule set** (65 µs to 2.3 ms, a 35× spread) because a join enumeration that yields no candidate is advanced without being charged — PLAN §M-P0's flat-cost corollary is amended, the decision it supported is not.
- **Phase 146 / M-P4b — the panel** (`lib/presentation/panels/proof_panel.dart`), two levels. The list offers what was *derived* and never a given: a hypothesis is what the figure was built to be, and thirty of them would bury the three statements the prover found. A selection narrows to statements mentioning **at least one** selected object — two selected points ask what relates them, and the answer usually names a third. Rendered from `Proof.steps`, never `render()`. Every one of the four states renders, including the two easy to leave blank: the refusal says why, and an exhausted run says it stopped and offers **Keep going**. Docked where there is room, a bottom sheet where there is not.
- **Phase 147 / M-P4c — the emphasis.** `proofHighlightProvider` carries one step's ids from the panel to the canvas, **deliberately not the selection**: one is what a command would act on, the other is what a sentence is about, and folding them would let a proof step silently retarget the next tool. The painter draws it as the selection halo's geometry at twice the width, under the selection pass, so an object that is both shows both rings. The pulse is handed to `CustomPainter`'s `repaint` — a tick repaints without rebuilding the canvas — and runs exactly while something is highlighted.
- **The wasm smoke now reaches the prover.** M-P4 is what puts it in `lib/main.dart`'s import graph, `event_loop_yield.dart`'s conditional import included — closing the gotcha Sessions 156 and 157 both carried.
- **Mutation-checked**: dropping the generation guard fails exactly the cleared-in-flight pin; dropping the Euclidean guard and dropping the `ProverRunning` publish each fail exactly their own test; ignoring `maxApplications` fails exactly the three cap tests.

**Next.** Merge `phase-145-mp4-proof-panel` — a **deploy**, so check `gh run list` after. Then the phase the measurements above earned: **the prover in an isolate on native, and the id-based fact transfer that needs**. M-P3 (the full-angle engine behind one `Prover` facade) still waits — nothing asks for a second engine. Carried: M-P4's own leftover, point-merge UX saying "these coincide by Varignon's theorem" with a *Show why* opening the panel; Phase 139's open box (decline an unaffordable detour); the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation; the direct/reflected `simtri` split.

**Gotchas.**

- **A rule-application budget bounds work, not time**, and the panel is where a user feels the difference. On a document in the blowup regime, **Keep going** buys another ~2 s and may still not finish. Nothing lies about it — `reachedFixpoint` is false and the row says "there may be more" — but if the prover ever needs to promise a wall-clock, it needs its own per-document measurement, not a bigger constant.
- **The provider does not watch the construction, on purpose**, so *every* consumer owes the staleness comparison. `isStale` in the panel is the only one today; a second consumer that forgets it will silently show a proof of a figure that has moved.
- **`hasRunningAnimations` is not a usable probe in this app** — the editor always has a ticker somewhere, so it reads true even with the pulse disabled entirely. The pulse test reads the value straight off the painter instead: the value moving *is* the animation. Worth remembering before writing the next animation test.
- **A `late final` reading `ref` runs its initializer wherever it is first touched** — which, for a panel nobody interacted with, is inside `dispose`, exactly where `ref` is unavailable. Read eagerly in `initState`. And the clear itself is post-frame, because a provider may not be modified inside a life-cycle at all.
- `DiagramFilter.probe` runs *before* the first yield in `prove()`, and must: it perturbs the live construction's roots and restores them bit-exactly, so nothing may interleave with it. It is also cheap (< 1 ms on every fixture measured) — the engine is the whole cost.

## Session 157 (V2 Session 59) — 2026-08-21

**Done — Phase 143 merged and deployed; Phase 144 / M-P2c: the proof walk, and the derivation check that closes last session's gotcha.** `phase-143-mp2b-dd-rules` merged to `main` (`67fef60`) and pushed. Phase 144 lives on `phase-144-mp2c-proof-dag`, unmerged, two commits. Suite **2913** green (2893 + 20), analyze clean, browser gate green (6).

- **The walk is small because Phase 142 paid for it in advance** (`proof.dart`). `Proof.of(goal, database)` takes the backward closure of one goal over the stored derivations — the sub-DAG that actually supports it, not everything the run happened to derive (pinned: the closure is strictly smaller than the database on the Varignon rig, and equals a support set computed independently of the walk). **Post-order, each fact once**: every citation points upwards, and a fact reached by two routes is stated once and cited twice. Iterative with an explicit stack — a 20 000-link chain is a test, not a stack overflow — and a cycle guard that throws, which needed a `FactDatabase` subclass to test at all, since the real store *cannot* be made to hold a cycle (premises-must-be-present plus first-insert-wins).
- **It reads like a proof.** `render()` is a numbered statement/reason list with points named the way the figure names them (`attributes.name`, id as fallback): `[3] para(A, C, M, N)  midline_para from [1], [2]`, seven lines for Varignon.
- **The phase's real content is `derivation_check.dart`, and it closes the gotcha Session 156 recorded.** The filter screens a *conclusion's truth* and structurally cannot say whether the premises recorded beside it entail it — so a mis-joining unifier that emitted a true, correctly-shaped conclusion passed "fact present", "derivation names the rule", and the screen, and would print as a proof of something it does not prove. `checkDerivation` re-matches a derivation against its own rule: recorded premises against premise patterns **in slot order**, bound over `orbitArguments` (the full orbit, because `cong(o,a,o,b)`'s repetition is exactly what canonical order sorts apart — `[a,o,b,o]`), under one binding whose conclusion canonicalizes to the recorded fact. `Proof.verify()` runs it over every deduction and answers the reasons, empty exactly when the proof is a certificate.
- **The unifier is restated, not shared.** A checker built on `rule_engine.dart`'s `_bind` would inherit the matcher's bugs instead of catching them; the duplication is the point and is said at the site.
- **A given is valid with nothing checked** — a hypothesis's warrant is the construction's parent ties, established one boundary earlier by `hypotheses()` and screened by the filter. This function adjudicates rule applications, and a given is not one.
- **PLAN's M-P2c bullet was amended first**, since "checked, not just printed" is a soundness claim and that is where soundness claims live.
- **Mutation-checked**: binding to the canonical spelling instead of the full orbit fails exactly three tests (the orbit pin, the sweep over a real fixpoint's derivations, the certificate test); dropping the conclusion-canonicalizes-to-the-record check fails exactly the two rejection tests that turn on it plus `verify`'s.

**Next — M-P4, the proof panel** (the user's call, taken at the end of this session; `phase-144-mp2c-proof-dag` merged to `main` before stopping, which closes M-P2). M-P4 over M-P3 because it is the milestone's *product* and the consumer that makes three deferrals real at once: `Isolate.run` wrapping, the id-based fact transfer facts-key-by-identity forces, and the wasm smoke finally reaching the prover's web arm. Shape, to be opened as Phase 145 next session and worth splitting: a provider running `hypotheses` → `seedHypotheses` → `runChunked` off the live construction and holding goal → `Proof` (the domain stays Flutter-free; the boundary is the provider); a `proof_panel.dart` step list rendered from `Proof.steps`, never from `render()`; then per-step on-figure highlighting reusing painter selection styling. M-P3 (the full-angle engine behind one `Prover` facade) waits — nothing asks for a second engine yet. Carried: Phase 139's open box (decline an unaffordable detour); the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation; the direct/reflected `simtri` split.

**Gotchas.**

- **`Proof.verify()` checks instantiation, not the rule table.** It answers "this step is a correct application of the rule it names" — if a *rule* in `ddCoreRules` is not a theorem, every proof using it verifies happily. That soundness rests on the per-rule numeric rigs in `rule_engine_test.dart`, which is the right place for it; the two checks are complements and neither subsumes the other.
- The walk's post-order is a reading choice, not the insertion order. Insertion order is also a valid topological order (premises are strictly older) but interleaves unrelated branches; post-order keeps a sub-proof contiguous. M-P4 should render from `steps`, not re-sort.
- Nothing in `lib/main.dart`'s import graph reaches the prover yet, so the wasm smoke still does not exercise any of it — unchanged from Session 156, and it stays true until M-P4.
- `checkDerivation`'s search is bounded by the rule table (three premises at most, orbit branching), not by the database — but it is exponential in premise count by shape, so a future rule with many `eqangle` premises would want a pruning order rather than the straight backtrack.

## Session 156 (V2 Session 58) — 2026-08-21

**Done — Phase 143 / M-P2b: hypotheses, the DD rule core, and the fixpoint that chunks through the channel.** On `phase-143-mp2b-dd-rules`, unmerged, six commits. Suite **2893** green (2842 + 51), analyze clean, browser gate green (+2), wasm build compiles.

- **Phase 142 merged and deployed first** (`3273a5f`) — all three workflows green, checked with `gh run list` this time.
- **Hypotheses are what the construction guarantees, read the way `incidence.dart` reads** (`hypotheses.dart`): incidence-shaped facts (coll/cyclic/radius-cong) over `structurallyIncident`, plus per-kind statements for every kind with a pointwise theorem — midpoints, the reflection family, projections, triangle centers (Centroid deliberately empty), bisector lines, Thales on a diameter circle, the compass transfer, the harmonic eqratio. Point-tuple vocabulary means a line's facts need witness pairs, so all combinations are emitted and the Fact keying collapses them. Euclidean-only, refused, the filter's argument one boundary earlier. **The test's spine: every emitted hypothesis must survive the filter on the rig that emitted it** — an emission the construction does not guarantee cannot land.
- **The 23 rules are data in the DD sources' own notation** (`rule.dart`, `'cong(o,a,o,b) & cong(p,a,p,b) => perp(o,p,a,b)'`), a conclusion only naming variables its premises bound. `simtri → eqangle` is deliberately absent: orientation-free `simtri` (M-P1's choice) admits the reflected pair, which negates every mod-π angle — the one place the missing direct/reflected split has consequences, said at the site.
- **The engine is semi-naive over the canonical store, and the matcher binds against the full orbit** (`orbitArguments`, now public beside `Fact`): a stored fact is one canonical spelling, and `cong(o,a,o,b)`'s repeated variable has to see the spelling the canonical order sorted away — within-segment swaps included, which the canonicalizer's reduced enumeration never enumerates (pinned: canonical == least of the full orbit). Each fact pivots once; joins run over the facts stored when its turn began; the per-kind index lives in the engine, the consumer M-P2a named for it.
- **Every candidate conclusion is screened through the filter, and that is also where the DD sources' ncoll side conditions live.** The test seeds a structurally-true collinear eqangle, watches `isosceles_converse` fire, and finds the false cong refused. Structural degeneracy (zero-length segments, repeated coll points, the identity triangle pair) is refused at binding time without spending budget.
- **The budget is rule applications** (Phase 140's unit, Phase 139's reason) and `step(1)` chunking is pinned bit-identical to a straight run. The fixpoint tests carry a depth-three chain (midp → coll + midline → intercept eqratio), the Varignon parallelogram via `para_transitive`, and premise-strictly-older-than-conclusion — the M-P2c walk's contract, now enforced end to end.
- **The MessageChannel yield has its home** (`event_loop_yield.dart` + `_vm`/`_web` arms, conditional import, never a capability check), and `ProverEngine.runChunked` is its first consumer. The browser gate holds the seam: `prover_yield_web_test` pins that the *shipped* yield shows no setTimeout clamp (0.033 ms vs the timer's 4.582 — the wrong arm is silent everywhere else) and that a chunked browser fixpoint derives the straight database. **The layer rule fired on the web arm — the gate working** — and was extended deliberately: `dart:async` allowlisted (as portable as core), `dart:js_interop` allowed only in `*_web.dart` arms so it can never leak into shared domain code that the VM compiles and throws on.
- **Deferred with the consumer named**: `Isolate.run` wrapping. Facts key by point *identity*, so crossing an isolate needs an id-based transfer, and that design belongs to the first UI consumer (M-P2c/M-P4), not on spec.
- **Mutation-checked**: dropping the orbit's transpose family fails exactly the two orbit pins plus the three matcher tests that genuinely need transpose spellings; removing the filter screen fails exactly the side-condition test.

**Next.** Merge `phase-143-mp2b-dd-rules` — a **deploy**, so check `gh run list` after. Then M-P2c: the proof DAG surfaced as a readable derivation (the premises-older invariant makes the walk cycle-free by construction). Carried: Phase 139's open box (decline an unaffordable detour); the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation; the direct/reflected `simtri` split if M-P2c's proofs want eqangle out of similarity.

**Gotchas.**

- **The filter's screen masks unsound *instantiation*, not just degenerate instances.** A broken unifier that mis-joins premises but happens to emit a true, correctly-shaped conclusion is invisible to every test that checks "conclusion present, derivation names the rule" — the screen only vouches for the conclusion's truth, never for the premises entailing it. The per-rule rigs pin each rule's *statement* numerically; instantiation validity rests on `_bind`'s clash check, which no test currently kills. Worth a dedicated pin if M-P2c's printed proofs make wrong-premise derivations visible.
- **A `tail -N` on a backgrounded gate throws away the failure.** The first full-suite run piped through `tail -4` and the one failing test's name was lost; the rerun had to capture the whole log. Redirect to a file first, filter after — the same lesson Phase 140 recorded for dart2js under a pipe.

The dart2js browser gate is the only target that compiles *and runs* `event_loop_yield_web.dart` today; when the prover gets a UI consumer, the wasm smoke starts covering it for free.
- `hypotheses()` emissions are all-pairs per carrier, deliberately: the DD core has no coll-propagation rules to reconstruct a missing spelling. On documents with many points glued to one line this is quadratic-to-quartic in points-per-carrier; fine for real documents, but the place to look first if extraction ever shows up in a profile.
- The midline fixpoint is small (10 facts, 44 applications) — eqangle blowup through zero-angle facts did not materialize on the tested rigs, but `eqangle`'s 128-form orbit makes joins the engine's cost center by construction. M-P2b's budget bounds it; no tuning was needed.

## Session 155 (V2 Session 57) — 2026-08-21

**Done — Phase 142 / M-P2a: the fact database, and the canonical form is an orbit under a group that has to be right.** On `phase-142-mp2a-fact-keying`, unmerged, two commits. Suite **2842** green (2799 + 43), analyze clean, browser gate green.

- **Phase 141 is merged** (`7db506d`) — STATUS 154 recorded it as unmerged; disk disagreed and disk won. `main` also carries two commits after it (the `tap()` warning silencing and a README note). Workflows green per the user; not re-checked.
- **M-P2 is split into three**, and the split is the session's first decision: keying / rules+fixpoint / proof surfacing. The whole of M-P2 is three sessions in one box, and the keying is what the other two are built on — a rule engine written against identity-compared predicates would have to be rewritten. PLAN §M-P2 now carries the split; 142 is M-P2a.
- **A `Fact` is a `Predicate` reduced to the least element of its orbit under the kind's own symmetry group**, ordered by point `id` — so a `Set<Fact>` *is* the database, and `para(a,b,c,d)` and `para(d,c,b,a)` are one fact. Without that, forward chaining never reaches quiescence: every rule rediscovers its conclusion in a new spelling forever. Ordering by id rather than by construction order is what keeps a printed proof reproducible.
- **The fiddly part PLAN named is `eqangle`/`eqratio`, and it is one group for both.** Both relate four segments in the shape `[[s1,s2],[s3,s4]]`, so both carry the dihedral group of the square, order **eight**: the row swap (the two sides of an equation commute), the column swap (negate both angles — exact, since angles between lines are read mod π — or invert both ratios), and the **transpose**, which is the one easy to miss and doubles the collapse: `θ2−θ1 ≡ θ4−θ3` rearranges to `θ3−θ1 ≡ θ4−θ2`, i.e. `∠(s1,s3)=∠(s2,s4)`; and `s1/s2=s3/s4` cross-multiplies to `s1/s3=s2/s4`.
- **The symmetry table is pinned against the evaluators, not against itself, and that is the phase's real invariant.** A group element that is not actually a symmetry merges two different statements and makes every proof downstream unsound — and the canonicalizer cannot be asked about it. So each kind gets a rig where its predicate is *true*, and every orbit form must still hold under 24 random similarities (which preserve the whole vocabulary when applied to all points at once) and must still fail on a broken rig. The other half is pinned too: eight plausible rewrites that are **not** symmetries must key apart and evaluate false.
- **`FactDatabase` carries provenance from day one** — a `Derivation` is a hypothesis, or a rule name over premises. Two enforced invariants, which are the same argument twice: **first insert wins** (a fact re-derived by a longer route keeps the derivation it was discovered with), and **a derivation may only cite facts already present** (`add` throws otherwise). Together every premise is strictly older than its conclusion, so the proof DAG M-P2c walks cannot contain a cycle and cannot reach a dangling edge — structural, not hoped for.
- **Deliberately absent, with the consumer named**: no index by kind (M-P2b's rule matcher would justify one; it does not exist), no rule objects, no hypothesis extraction from a `Construction`, no coupling to `DiagramFilter` — screening candidates stays the filter's job; the database holds what a rule actually derived.
- **Mutation-checked** against a committed baseline, each mutation failing exactly its own tests: dropping the transpose family → the two eight-point collapse tests; dropping the triangle swap → `simtri`/`contri`; dropping the pair swap → the three four-point ones plus the `Set` test; sorting `midp`'s three arguments → its non-symmetry test; last-insert-wins and an unchecked premise → their own. And one mutation of the *test*: adding a one-angle reversal to the test's own orbit table fails the numeric pin, which is the evidence that pin has teeth rather than merely agreeing with the implementation.

**Next.** Merge `phase-142-mp2a-fact-keying` — a **deploy**, so check `gh run list` after. Then M-P2b: hypotheses read off the construction, the ~20-rule DD core from the open DDAR/Newclid sources, screened through `DiagramFilter`, run to quiescence in Phase 140's resumable shape with a rule-application budget. Carried: the `MessageChannel` yield needs its `dart:js_interop` home the moment M-P2b's engine actually chunks — that is now the *next* phase, not a distant one; Phase 139's open box (decline an unaffordable detour); the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation.

**Gotchas.**

- **A canonicalizer cannot be tested against itself, and it is the most tempting thing in this phase to do.** Asserting that the orbit collapses only says the implementation agrees with the test's table; both can be wrong the same way. The test that carries the weight is the numeric one — every form the table claims is the same statement must *evaluate* the same — and its own mutation (adding a non-symmetry to the test table) is what shows it does.
- **The eight-point group is easy to get to four.** Row swap and column swap are obvious and generate only the Klein four-group; the transpose is what makes it eight, and forgetting it does not fail loudly — it just leaves half of every eqangle/eqratio conclusion stored twice, and M-P2b's fixpoint would be slower for no visible reason.
- **`simtri`/`contri` admit any permutation of the corners only when it is applied to *both* triples.** The correspondence is the statement; permuting one triangle alone is a different claim, and it is in the non-symmetry list for that reason.
- Ordering by `id` presupposes ids being unique, which `Construction` guarantees. Equality is still by point *identity*, so a duplicated id degrades the ordering without ever merging two points — pinned.
- `Derivation`'s rule is a bare `String` the database never interprets. If M-P2b wants a richer reference, it goes in there and nothing in the store changes.

## Session 154 (V2 Session 56) — 2026-08-20

**Done — Phase 141 / M-P1: the predicate vocabulary and the numeric filter, sampled per diagram rather than per predicate.** On `phase-141-mp1-predicates`, unmerged, four commits. Suite **2799** green (2756 + 43), analyze clean, browser gate green, wasm build compiles.

- **`lib/domain/prover/` opens** with the ten DD predicates (`coll / para / perp / cong / cyclic / eqangle / eqratio / midp / simtri / contri`): `PredicateKind` with fixed arities (enforced as a programmer-error contract, the `LineEq` pattern), numeric evaluators as pure chart functions on dimensionless or scale-relative residuals against one 1e-6 tolerance — deliberately the coincidence probe's magnitude, and for its argument: identical predicates compute to float noise, accidents broken by a probe separate by ~`probeScale`.
- **The filter's shape is the phase's main design decision: configurations are sampled once, predicates are answered many times.** `DiagramFilter.probe` perturbs every mutable root, recomputes, snapshots every point's position, restores bit-exactly — `probeCount` (3) times up front — and `holds(predicate)` reads the stored snapshots. M-P2's forward chaining screens *every candidate deduction* against this filter, so the recompute cost had to be per diagram, not per predicate.
- **The probe machinery is `point_coincidence.dart`'s, extracted rather than duplicated**: `lib/domain/construction/mutable_roots.dart` (collect roots / perturb-from-base / bit-exact restore / `recomputeCarriers`), with the coincidence probe re-pointed at it — same constants, same RNG draw order, its 10 tests untouched.
- **Euclidean-only, refused rather than approximated**: the vocabulary measures in the Euclidean chart, so `probe` throws on a proper absolute. A CK prover re-founds the predicates at this boundary — the same place M-CK re-founded measurement — rather than inheriting chart answers about a figure in a different geometry.
- **Degeneracies answer conservatively, and the one that slipped through is worth naming**: `cyclic` via inscribed angles guards every coincidence its denominators name, but c ≡ d makes the test compare an angle with its own copy — vacuously true with no zero anywhere in the formula. Caught by its test; the guard now enumerates the pairs, not the denominators.
- `simtri`/`contri` are orientation-free (side ratios / SSS), admitting reflected triangles; Newclid's direct/reflected split goes beside the ratio check if M-P2's rules need it, said at the site. Canonical forms and fact keying (two `eqangle`s naming one fact) are deliberately absent — `Predicate` compares by identity and says so; that is M-P2's fact database.
- **The unchecked push from session 153 had in fact failed, and the failure was a flaky assertion in the Phase 140 yield test.** CI on the docs commit (`437d527`) lost `yield_cost_web_test`'s microtask-vs-channel ordering: 0.0392 vs 0.037 ms — two unclamped primitives photo-finishing inside scheduling noise on a shared runner, with the finding itself (timer 4.128 ms, 112× the channel) holding comfortably. The strict ordering was never load-bearing — the microtask is rejected because it never returns to the event loop, not for its cost — so the assertion now pins the property that matters (the microtask shows no clamp either, `< timerMs / 2`) and says at the site why it is not ordered against the channel. Fixed on this branch, so the merge carries it.

**Next.** Merge `phase-141-mp1-predicates` — a **deploy**, so check `gh run list` after. Then M-P2 (forward chaining: fact database on canonical predicate forms, the ~20-rule DD core, fixpoint with the Phase 140 resumable shape and rule-application budget, proof DAG for free). Carried: the `MessageChannel` yield needs its `dart:js_interop` home when the M-P2 engine first chunks; Phase 139's open box (decline an unaffordable detour); the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation.

**Gotchas.**

- **A formula's guards are not its degeneracies.** `cyclic`'s coincidence guards came from the four direction vectors in the formula, and the one coincident pair (c, d) that zeroes *no* vector is exactly the one that makes the test vacuous. When a check's degenerate cases are enumerated from its algebra, also enumerate them from its arguments.
- The filter's `holds` throws on a predicate over a point outside the diagram rather than answering false — misuse is a programmer error, not a numeric outcome, and swallowing it would hide M-P2 bugs. But an *undefined* point (in any configuration) is false, not a throw: that is a real state of the diagram.
- `Predicate.holdsNow` is one configuration, no perturbation — useful in tests and for M-P4's always-on cheap probe someday, but never evidence of a fact. The filter is the only thing that speaks for the diagram.
- The extraction preserved `point_coincidence`'s RNG draw order (free points then parameters, per probe) deliberately; its default `Random(57)` outcomes are unchanged. If `MutableRoots.perturb` ever reorders its loops, that determinism moves.
- `flutter build web --wasm` was run this session and compiles, but the prover code is pure `dart:math` Dart — the layer-rule test is what actually guards its portability.

## Session 153 (V2 Session 55) — 2026-08-20

**Done — Phase 140 / M-P0: the threading decision, and the thing that decides it is the yield primitive, which the plan never named.** On `phase-140-mp0-threading`, **merged and pushed** (`11fa132`). Suite **2756** green (unchanged — a spike adds no `lib/` code), analyze clean, browser gate green and now carrying two more tests. Phase 139 merged and pushed to `main` at `d98f103` at the user's direction, without a `gh run list` check.

- **Scoped as a spike, in the Phase 101 sense**, and that framing is load-bearing: PLAN requires the decision "before any prover code", not an engine. There is nothing to drive yet, and an abstraction with no consumer is what this project keeps declining to build. The deliverable is three measurements and a pinned decision.
- **The prior held in every part except one, and the exception is the whole phase.** "Resumable state machine, cooperatively chunked on web, `Isolate.run` on native, Worker-portable later" is right. What it did not name is *how* the web engine yields — and the Dart-idiomatic answer is a 105× trap.
- **`await Future.delayed(Duration.zero)` is `setTimeout`, and setTimeout is clamped.** The HTML spec mandates a 4 ms floor once five timeouts have nested. Measured in Chrome: **5.07 ms/yield**, against **0.048 ms** for a `MessageChannel` `postMessage` round trip — a macrotask, so the browser still renders, but not a timer, so nothing clamps it. On the synthetic fixpoint the difference is not tuning: chunking a 109 ms run at a 1 000-application budget takes **16.8 s** on dart2wasm, and dart2js never finishes. Through the channel, sixty 4 ms chunks in a browser showed **no measurable scheduling overhead**.
- **That number could only be taken in a browser, and finding that out cost most of the session.** Node clamps setTimeout to ~1 ms rather than 4, and — worse — **dart2js under Node exits silently part-way through a microtask-yield loop**, exit code 0, output truncated because dart2js buffers stdout to a pipe. Two separate Node artifacts, both of which read as real findings until checked. `test/web/` is where the measurement belongs, for exactly the reason Phase 126d created it.
- **The resumable shape is cheap and budget-independent**: 10% VM, 2% AOT, 11% dart2js, 4% dart2wasm over the straight-line loop, flat from 1 000 to 500 000 rule applications per step. So it is what the prover should be *everywhere* — the native isolate wraps it rather than replacing it, which is also what keeps "Worker-portable later" real. Its budget is counted in rule applications, not milliseconds, for Phase 139's reason exactly: bound a resumable walk by work, and it stays a deterministic function of its input.
- **`Isolate.run` on native is close to free**: 0.05–0.09 ms round trip, ~4 ns/int to bring a fact database home, 0.16–0.22 ms of boundary on a 75–91 ms job (1.00×). The rule that follows from the numbers rather than taste: export a job only when it is longer than the round trip.
- **`dart:isolate` compiles cleanly for both web targets and throws at runtime** (`UnsupportedError: new RawReceivePort` on dart2js, a trap on dart2wasm). Recorded as a rule, because the failure mode is worse than a build error: the native path must be reached by conditional import, and the browser gate is the only thing that can catch a violation.
- **The Web Worker is rejected for now, and the write-up says exactly how far the evidence goes.** Not needed — chunking cleared the bar it would have been introduced to clear. *Not verified* to work under dart2wasm; that was not measured, and saying so is better than deferring it as folklore.

**Next.** M-P1 (predicates + the numeric filter, generalizing `point_coincidence.dart`'s perturbation probe from "same position" to "predicate survives perturbation"), which M-P0 no longer blocks. Carried: the Android/iOS smokes (environment-blocked), Phase 139's named open box (a pass should decline a detour it cannot afford rather than pay and fail), Phase 134's uncontested locus-chain exclusion. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation.

**Gotchas.**

- **A benchmark harness can be the wrong instrument, and say so only by being weird.** dart2js/Node exiting silently mid-`await` is not a Dart fact or a web fact; it is a Node fact. The tell was an exit code of 0 with half the output missing. When a measurement on a surrogate platform looks impossible rather than merely bad, suspect the surrogate before the subject.
- **dart2js buffers stdout to a pipe**, so `timeout N cmd | tail` loses everything on kill. Redirect to a file when a run might not finish.
- **The warmup in the yield test is load-bearing, not hygiene.** The timer clamp engages only after five nested timeouts, so a measurement that does not first nest deeply enough reports setTimeout's *unclamped* cost and misses the finding entirely. It is commented as such at the site.
- The first synthetic fixpoint saturated a 65 536-fact space and took minutes; the modulus is the only dial on run length (work is ~3·N²) and is now set so the baseline lands in the tens of milliseconds. Convergence-by-saturation is the intended shape — a real DD run also converges because most applications rediscover something.
- `Stopwatch.elapsedMicroseconds` on dart2js has millisecond resolution — its columns print as whole milliseconds. Fine at these magnitudes, misleading at smaller ones.

- **Phase 138 and Phase 136b are both merged, pushed and green** — 138's three workflows all succeeded, 136b's were in flight at session start and are the tail of `main`. STATUS 151's "check `gh run list` after" is closed.
- **The decision, taken as Phase 134 asked for it: a budget that scales with the graph.** `TracingFlags.dragStepBudget` becomes an `int?` **pin** (null by default) and the budget a pass gets is `dragStepBudgetWork ~/ objects-recomputed-per-trial`, clamped to [128, 2048]. The derivation is captured once per gesture — the graph is what it divides and a drag cannot change the graph — while the *pin* is read per frame, because a caller setting it mid-drag means it now (that is exactly what `drag_session_tracing_test`'s mid-gesture `= 0` bail switch does, and capturing it would have silently broken that test rather than failing it).
- **The work quota is the shipped constant restated: 12288 = 128 × 96**, the gate rig's downstream count. So the construction the constant was tuned on gets 128 trials exactly as before, and everything else moves in proportion to what its trials actually cost. The invariant bought is that a **starving frame's marginal cost is now constant across graph sizes** instead of linear in them.
- **The old number was wrong, and correcting it is half the phase.** "One trial is ~0.08 ms on the 100-object stress rig" — the figure behind "128 is already ~10 ms of starving frame and 512 would be ~40", which is why 512 was rejected across two phases — was a **frame** cost measured at 1.00 trials/frame. It charges every trial with the once-per-pass dependent hoist and slot seeding. The marginal trial is ~7× cheaper. The *shape* of the objection survives (a constant is still wrong); the arithmetic did not.
- **Measured, on a starving frame, across a 32× range of graph sizes** (`benchmark/drag_budget_bench.dart` + `run_drag_budget.sh`, all four targets). Per-trial cost is flat **per downstream object** — 0.33 µs VM, 0.45 dart2js, 0.52 dart2wasm, 0.64 AOT — and that flatness is the whole justification for a work quota. Getting the rig right mattered: a path that merely *crosses* a degeneracy detours in ~66 trials at every graph size and measures nothing about the budget at all, so the harness ends its path **on** the singularity, where no arc can enclose it and the controller creeps until it gives up.
- **The payoff is that a test stopped hand-tuning.** `drag_crossing_test`'s crossing at B was pinned at a hand-set `dragStepBudget = 512` with a comment anticipating this phase; it now carries at the derived budget with nothing set, against a new control at the old constant that still loses G to the driver's own root. The frame straddling the carrier collapse at t = 0 is also now *crossed* instead of bailed on — pinned, and the bail-path test is explicitly pinned back to 128 so that path keeps its coverage.
- **The locus-exclusion rule now lives in one place.** `Construction._tracedPartition` splits the dependents into the core a trial recomputes and the loci a pass settles once (117b), and both the walk and the new public `tracedWorkPerTrial` read it — so they cannot drift. Counting a locus would shrink the budget for work no trial does.
- **Mutation-checked** against a committed baseline: reverting the derivation to the constant fails exactly the four Phase 139 tests and nothing else; counting loci as per-trial work fails exactly its own.

**Next.** Merge `phase-139-drag-step-budget` — a **deploy**, so check `gh run list` after. Phase 134 is then closed but for the locus-chain exclusion, which is uncontested. Then M-P0 (the threading benchmark PLAN puts before any prover code) and the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation; and the new open box below.

**Gotchas.**

- **A benchmark can measure the wrong regime and look fine doing it.** The first cut drove a path *through* the tangency; every graph size reported 66 trials and a flat ~3 ms, and the budget column plateaued identically at 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048. That is not a budget measurement — it is a successful detour, measured six times. Only a path that ends on the singularity starves, and starving is the case the budget bounds.
- **The per-object cost is target-dependent by 2×, and the slowest target is the one that ships to phones.** Calibrating on the VM alone would have understated the quota's time cost by half (~4.0 ms VM against ~7.8 AOT). Pinning the quota to the *gate rig's shipped budget* rather than to a millisecond target is what keeps it today's worst case on every target instead of a new one — worth remembering the next time a constant wants calibrating.
- **`flutter test` over two files in one invocation shares state that separate invocations do not.** A mutation run across `drag_crossing_test.dart` + `tracing_flags_test.dart` appeared to fail a Phase 134 test; run alone it passed. `TracingFlags` is process-global mutable state, so mutation results are only trustworthy per-file. (The apparent extra failure was also partly a misread `grep -B1`, which prints the preceding line — check what a filter is actually showing before believing a surprising row.)
- A starving frame at the shipped budget **already** exceeds the 8 ms gate on AOT once a locus is in the graph (16.4 ms measured). Unchanged by this phase and not introduced by it; the gate is defined on the steady-state frame, which is 1–19% of budget. Named as the new open box: on a graph large enough for the floor to bind, a pass pays time linear in the graph *and* has too few trials to finish a detour, so it pays and fails. The fix is to decline a detour it cannot afford, not to raise a number.

It is, and `_structuralIndex`'s shared-role path never depended on how many roots remain; there was simply no test. Rig: two ellipses, `x²/9 + y²/4 = 1` and `x²/4 + y²/9 = 1`, crossing at (±s, ±s) with P = (s, s) a defining point of both. Wiggling P: the named crossing is P at **0 of 720** samples; the same geometry with the incidence hidden behind a ratio-1 homothety (the Phase 135/136b technique) loses it at **359 of 720**, arriving by a **3.4**-unit jump. **Mutation-checked**: refusing the shared role above one leftover degrades the named point to exactly 359 of 720 — the two numbers meet, which is the cleanest form this evidence could take.
- **And the refusal is right and not free.** With three roots left over there is nothing unique to divide out, so the refusal stands; each of the three then exchanges by the same ~3.4 over the same wiggle. That is now pinned as the baseline any future widening has to beat, rather than left as "untested either way".
- **The widening box is worked to the edge of its own policy.** `onCarrierDefiningPoints` lists a curve's defining points that lie on its own carrier, and **`DiameterCircle` was the one curve kind with that property missing from it** — a gap in the switch's stated remit, not speculative widening, since every sibling with the property was already there. Added, and pinned on the Thales configuration: a chord through one end of the diameter has that end as a crossing at every position of its other parent, so the two roles split cleanly. Audited the rest: compass / fixed-radius / Apollonius / triangle circles, the polar and radical-axis lines and the focal conic are all correctly absent. What is left is genuinely new `_derivedIncident` theorems — a `Midpoint` or `SegmentRatioPoint` on the line through its own parents, a `ProjectionPoint` on its reference line — which is exactly what "as real documents call for it" defers, and none was added on spec.

**Next.** Merge `phase-136b-four-candidate-pin` — a **deploy**, so check `gh run list` after; Phase 138's deploy is also unverified. Then the `dragStepBudget` decision in Phase 134 (measurements already in hand: ~90 trials to classify starvation, ~20 for the arc, ~40 for the exit against a budget of 128, and one trial is ~0.08 ms on the stress rig — the open question is a budget that scales with the graph rather than a constant). Then M-P0 and the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation.

**Gotchas.**

- **The STATUS 148 "commit before mutation-testing" gotcha caught this session, exactly as written.** `git checkout lib/domain/construction/incidence.dart` after a mutation reverted to HEAD — which predated the *uncommitted* `DiameterCircle` addition — and silently clobbered it. Cheap to redo here; the rule is to commit the implementation before mutating, not merely to have *a* baseline.
- **A probe path can degenerate the rig rather than exercise it.** Swinging P a full circle of radius s about the origin drove it through the other defining points, so the conics left general position and both probes went null for stretches — noise, not evidence. A local wiggle (radius 0.35) keeps all five points in general position and moves only the crossings, which is what the question was about.
- Deflation's branch numbering is not "0 is the leftover": on the Thales rig branch 0 *is* the shared point. Tests here find the two roles by `tracksDeflatedRoot` rather than assuming an index — the first draft assumed and failed.
- The 720-sample sweeps in `deflation_test.dart` are each a full construction recompute per step; the group runs in well under a second at present scale, but it is the most expensive group in that file by an order of magnitude.

The reflection fixes A and C so |AD| = |AB| and |CD| = |CB|, and |CB| = |AB| already because C rides the compass circle about B. **The argument was in the repo a phase early** — `mirrorPointAcross`'s own doc says circle-crossing reflections swap when the mirrored point crosses the axis, and it applies here verbatim.

It pins reachability and the command path, which is what the standing rule asks of it; the 600-sample sweep pins the geometry. Both are needed and they pin different things.

- **The design**: every line kind's stored representative sign now carries its orientation; `ProjLine.toOrientedLineEq` projects it sign-preservingly (`toLineEq` scrambles the sign through `normalized` — that is what the per-kind `orientedAlong` re-anchors were compensating for); `orientedAlong` and every `_v1Direction()` are deleted. Underneath sits a new **point-representative contract: `w.re > 0` when exactly real and finite** (`ProjPoint.wPositive`), because a join's chart direction is `w₁w₂·(p₂ − p₁)` — orientation flows covariantly, so it is well-founded exactly when the roots of the flow are. Kernels stay multilinear; the signs live at the kind level, computed from projective data. Recorded in PLAN §"Orientation is the representative's sign", with where the sign discontinuities now live (states where the object degenerates or V1 never spoke, instead of chart events grid snapping lands on).
- **Measured before believed, twice.** The w-contract gate over both corpora × three absolutes found eight offender kinds; a 5000-pair probe found **41% of real finite line∩conic candidates arrive with `w < 0`**. And the four kinds' σ fixes looked like dead code against every fixture — `CircleEq.lift` always emits a positive trace — until the real source surfaced: **`ThreePointCircle`'s circumcircle determinant emits `tr Q < 0` for clockwise-wound points**, the same circle with the opposite representative. That is what 136c's "roughly half of configurations" was. Each σ fix is mutation-checked against a clockwise circumcircle.
- **A lesson worth keeping: deriving the chart from the representative makes rep-chart agreement gates tautological.** The inverted 136c eleven-kind gate passed with a σ fix removed, because chart and rep can no longer disagree *by construction*. Every pin that matters is against independent data: the v1 oracle's oriented lines, parent positions (the join's p1→p2 promise, pinned through an `IntersectionPoint` parent — the config the w-contract exists for), and circumcircle windings.
- **Branch addressing is chart-free**: the Phase 110 ordering re-anchor in `intersectionCandidates` deleted as a provable identity; `TangentLine`'s touch order is `det[adj(A)·ℓ∞, p, t]` scaled to be exactly the chart cross product, so the comparison is V1's and not merely its sign — and it now orders tangents to a chartless carrier (ellipse from an outside pole, pinned), where solver order previously stood.
- The five tests pinning the old doctrine ("representative signs are no kind's contract") updated to state the new one; complex-rescaling invariance of chart *orientation* narrowed to sign-preserving rescales (a complex phase legitimately carries into the representative; tracing is unaffected because the chart evaluator builds `w = 1` by hand, Phase 132c).
- `StubProjectiveLine.line` is oriented now, like every real kind. `dart format` also caught `conic_shape_test.dart` shipped unformatted in 132d (own commit).

**Next.** Merge `phase-137-line-orientation` — a **deploy**, so check `gh run list` after. Then the rhombus macro un-refusal (Phase 129/132's last box, a small tool change: `RhombusMacroTool.availableUnder` + the Euclid I.1 route for corner D + the toolbar row). Carried over: the two 136b boxes, the `dragStepBudget` decision in Phase 134, M-P0, the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation.

**Gotchas.**

- **The compat edge is narrow and stated in the phase**: a v2 document with a `TangentLine` on a chartless carrier, or a point glued to a polar/radical-axis/tangent of a general conic, had its branch/orientation decided by a solver artifact or `normalized`'s largest-coefficient sign; both are now specified and may settle differently on one reload. Euclidean documents are unaffected — every orientation V1 spoke is preserved, oracle-pinned.

- The tangent's `_leftness` divides the determinant by the rows' `w`s rather than multiplying by their signs — the *comparison* between the two touches must be V1's (each touch has a different `|w|` factor), not just each sign.

## Session 148 (V2 Session 50) — 2026-08-20

**Done — Phase 132d: the pencil angle is an address, and the settlement is `branchIndex`'s, not the one the phase was opened with.** On `phase-132d-pencil-angle-stability`, unmerged, three commits. Suite **2713** green (2701 + 12), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green.

- **The written design sketch was worked through and rejected, and that is the session's finding.** "Re-express on each recompute from the point's previous value" fails twice: unconditional continuation drifts *first-order under pure translation* (each step projects the old point onto the new curve instead of riding the frame — over a drag the point crawls off the figure), and any parameter mutation inside `recompute` is left behind by the locus sweep's restore (which restores only the driver's parameter), survives `point_coincidence`'s perturb-and-restore, and breaks bitwise undo. `recompute` stays a pure function — the exception the TODO asked to settle never materializes. The **gesture** holds identity, the **command** adopts it: `_GlueTracker` beside `_BranchSnapshot`, `GlueChange` beside `BranchChange`.
- **Switch detection is exact, and the mechanism is bitwise membership.** `ConicShape.frameSeeds` recomputes the base-point candidates by the factory's own operations, so the canonical choice is bitwise an element; "did the frame jump?" is an element comparison, no world-space tolerance anywhere. `carryParameterFrom` hands the angle back *identically* when nothing switched (pinned: 200 steps of a rotating near-circle, all bitwise no-ops), and at a switch replays the old frame on the new matrix so the named point does not move. Measured on the 146 rig: fixed angle jumps **4.4** world units, carried angle moves **0.034**.
- **The polar ↔ pencil boundary was found on the way and is covered**: a `FivePointConic` drifting through the `CircleEq` projection tolerance flips the parameter's *meaning* (polar angle ↔ pencil angle) — a latent jump nobody had reported. The tracker carries the point's position across that flip.
- Session-level coverage per the standing rule (engine tests miss the command path): a rigid translation and a single-point drag each cross a *probed* switch; continuity, command adoption, bitwise cancel/undo/redo, and no-switch-no-changes all pinned. Every guard mutation-checked — removing the per-frame reanchor or either command's undo replay fails exactly its own tests.

**Next.** Merge `phase-132d-pencil-angle-stability` — a **deploy**, so check `gh run list` after. Phase 132 is then fully closed and the rhombus macro (Phase 129) is unblocked — its un-refusal is a small tool change of its own. Then the phase Phase 136c opened — re-founding line orientation projectively. Carried over: the two 136b boxes, the `dragStepBudget` decision in Phase 134, M-P0, the Android/iOS smokes. Standing and deferred: the user's preferred **pure** route for deflation.

**Gotchas.**

- **An origin-centred rotated ellipse never switches frames** — its balanced frame is exact (`poleOf(ℓ∞)` is the zero vector bitwise), so every discrete choice is stable and the rig cannot exercise the carry at all. The first headline test asserted `switches > 0` and got 0. Probe rigs for this machinery need a *generic* centre; (0.4, 0.7) separates 130× (fixed 4.4 vs carried 0.034).
- **The there-and-back carry is not bitwise** — the two carries run on matrices one step apart, so the residue is the frame drift over the step (6.3e-4 measured, second order). Bitwise restoration is the session snapshot's job and the tests say so; don't "fix" the arithmetic toward a tolerance it cannot meet.

Mutations want a committed baseline so reverting a mutation is `git checkout` with nothing else in flight.
