## Session 132 (V2 Session 34) — 2026-08-18

**Done — Phase 126e: the two silent re-addressings, said out loud.** On `phase-126e-load-report`, two commits. The checklist line was "wire the decoder's `repairedIntersections` report into the place the geometry switch already reports"; it turned out to be two reports rather than one, and one of them did not exist yet. Suite **2532** green (from 2520), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, browser gate green.

- **An intersection point's *address* can change without the user touching it in exactly two ways**, and until now neither of them was fully spoken. Opening a document the reader had to repair, and switching the document's geometry, where `branchIndex` names a position in the candidate list *as ordered against the absolute*. Both are invisible by construction — a re-pointed crossing looks exactly like the one the user tapped — and a silent repair is how the Phase 120c document accumulated points nobody could account for in the first place. So they now share one report in one place.
- **The reader was silent about the case it cannot fix, which is the case that matters more.** `_separateDuplicateBranches` returned the ids it *moved*; a duplicate whose curve pair has no crossing left to give it fell out of the loop with nothing recorded at all. A repaired point is a defect the reader fixed and the user need only know something moved. An unrepaired one is still there, no drag can separate it, and the only remaining fix is to delete the surplus — which is the user's call and not the decoder's, so the honest move is to *say so*. `DecodedDocument.unrepairedIntersections`, the same shape as `GeometryChange.unmatched` and for the same reason.
- **`intersection_report.dart`: the sentence is the feature.** Both messages are plain functions taking no `BuildContext`, so what they *say* is testable without a widget tree, plus one `showIntersectionReport` so the two read as the same kind of news. The switch's phrasing is unchanged; `GeometryMenu._report` became a call to it.
- **Checked while wiring `main.dart`**: `decoded.kernel` is never applied on the load path and should not be. The kernel rides on `decoded.construction`, which the codec built with it; reading the field here would be a second source for one fact.
- **Tested at three levels — the Phase 126b lesson applied rather than restated.** The strings; that `showIntersectionReport` actually puts one on screen; and both real flows end to end — Open through the fake picker `file_menu_test` already had, and the switch through the `geometry_switch_test` witness (two tier-1 conics that miss, so the conics are bit-identical in both geometries, nothing moves, and only the numbering changes). Every one of the four Phase 126b–d defects was a correct value that no test drove all the way to the screen.
- **And the negative on each**, because "says nothing" is a claim here and not an omission: a well-formed document and an ordinary switch produce no snack bar at all.

**Also established this session (not code)** — the Pages deploy is live and green. `890fa45` ("Change github pages base href") had no STATUS entry; its CI, benchmark and deploy runs all succeeded, `https://stevesuave.github.io/regula2/` serves 200 with `base href="/regula2/"`, and `main.dart.wasm` (2.57 MB) is being served. Phase 122's last box stays **unticked**: the artifact is confirmed, an actual browser loading and driving the app is not, and that is what the box says.

**Next**
- Merge `phase-126e-load-report`.
- The **wasm browser smoke** is now one manual step rather than a blocked one — open the deployed URL, confirm the wasm build boots and a construction can be drawn.
- Phase 125/126's standing debt, unchanged: per-kind CK correctness beyond angle, and **`RotatedPoint`**, which is a gap rather than a refusal.
- `visibleWorldBounds` contributes nothing for a CK circle (it reads centre and radius, which a bitangent conic has not got), so fit-to-construction under-frames a hyperbolic document.
- **The absolute's radius is fixed at 1 world unit** — still written down rather than built, and still the thing that makes switching an existing Euclidean figure meaningless.

**Gotchas**
- **A report that is computed and discarded is worse than one that was never written**, because the code reads as though the obligation is met. `repairedIntersections` had a correct value, a docstring explaining why the user must be told, and no reader, for six phases. Grep for the *consumers* of a diagnostic, not for the diagnostic.
- **When a repair routine has an unhappy path, the unhappy path is the report.** The loop that finds a free crossing and the branch where there is none looked symmetrical; only one of them recorded anything. The give-away was in the existing docstring — it already explained that a surplus point "keeps its index and stays a duplicate", which is a user-visible outcome described in a comment and nowhere else.
- **`file_picker` 11 already has a test seam and I nearly added a second one.** `FilePickerPlatform.instance` is overridable and `test/presentation/file_menu_test.dart` has faked it since the file menu shipped; I had written an injectable opener onto `EditorScreen` before finding it. Production API added for testability is worth one grep through the existing tests first.
- **The `choose` helper in `geometry_menu_test` warns on every tap** ("A call to tap() … ignoring all but last") and has since Phase 126. Pre-existing and benign — the switch demonstrably happens — but it makes a genuinely missed tap unreadable in that file's output.
- The unrepairable case gets a **six-second snack bar for a defect the user has to act on**. Nobody has hit it in the wild; if anyone does, it wants a dialog naming the ids, or the object tree flagging them. Left as a TODO rather than built on speculation.

## Session 131 (V2 Session 33) — 2026-08-18

**Done — Phase 126b: three user-reported defects, all in the presentation boundary.** The user tried the new mode and reported that a triangle in the hyperbolic plane measured exactly 180°, that no circles appeared, and that elliptic looked identical to Euclidean. All three were real. Suite **2510** green, analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical.

- **The angle label read the marker, not the measure.** `labelText` took `angle.measure` — the chart sweep of the drawn arc — where it meant `GeoAngle.measure`. A triangle now labels 152° / 180° / 202° across the three geometries. This was also the whole of "elliptic looks the same as Euclidean".
- **Every Cayley–Klein circle was invisible.** `GeoCircle.isDefined` was `circle != null`, which asks whether the conic is a *Euclidean* circle. A CK circle is bitangent to the absolute, so `toCircleEq` answers null, so `isDefined` was false, so the painter skipped it — before ever reaching the conic arm that would have drawn it correctly. The conic was right the whole time.
- **The plane was two pixels across.** The absolute is the unit circle in *world* units; the default scale is one pixel per world unit. So the entire hyperbolic plane was a dot at the origin while the user's figure sat hundreds of units outside it — outside the plane, where the angles collapse to zero. `fittedToAbsolute` frames the disc on switching.
- **And that split one constant into two.** Framing needed `maxScale` above 50, which moved six goldens — the useful part of the whole exercise. One number was answering two different questions: how far the *user* may zoom (a hyperbolic document needs a lot) and how far a *fit* may blow a tiny figure up (unchanged). Now `maxScale` = 2000 and `maxFitScale` = 50.

**Then — Phase 126c, reported straight after.** "The hyperbolic circle has the same background colour as the outer space." The wash was drawn and in the right shape; it was one alpha of **0.09** on the absolute's colour, which measures a 5 % luminance step over the light canvas. Replaced by a per-theme colour with its alpha baked in — the two canvases start from opposite ends, so the alpha that is barely a tint over white is a large lift over near-black. Light and dark now measure 0.14 and 0.10. Suite **2514** green.

**Then — Phase 126d, and this was the real one.** Reported as "the hyperbolic circle has the same background colour as the outer space unless I zoom enough to have some part of the circumference off screen", which is a *precise* description of the defect and which I misread twice before screenshots settled it. `Path.combine(PathOperation.difference, rect, oval)` returns the outer rect **unbroken** on the web renderer when the oval is entirely contained, and the correct annulus as soon as any part crosses an edge. On the VM it is always correct. So a hyperbolic document showed a uniformly washed canvas with the rim floating on it, and repaired itself when the user zoomed out past the boundary.

- Reproduced directly: `flutter test --platform chrome` prints `difference-hole=false` where the VM prints `true`.
- Both uses are **even-odd fills** now, which express the same region with no boolean op and so have no implementation left to differ. The second use was the region-pick scrim — same shape, same containment, unreported and one grep away.
- **`test/web/` and a CI browser gate**, which is the finding that outlives the bug: web is the compile target, the VM harness is not the renderer, and nothing in the suite had ever run in a browser. The file keeps the boolean difference as evidence, asserting the broken behaviour, so the day an engine fixes it the test says so.
- Suite **2520** green + 2 on chrome, 32 goldens byte-identical.

**Next**
- Merge `phase-126b-ck-presentation` and `phase-126c-absolute-contrast`.
- **The absolute's radius is fixed at 1 world unit**, which is canonical and is a usability problem: an existing Euclidean construction is entirely outside the plane the moment it is switched. A per-document radius (`diag(1, 1, −1/R²)`, additive to the kernel block) would make switching an existing figure meaningful. It is a schema decision, so it is written down rather than built.
- `visibleWorldBounds` contributes nothing for a CK circle, so fit-to-construction under-frames a hyperbolic document.
- Unchanged: the load-time `repairedIntersections` report; per-kind CK correctness beyond angle; `RotatedPoint`; the wasm browser smoke.

**Gotchas**
- **A domain coverage gate cannot see the screen, and that is where this milestone actually failed.** `ck_kind_coverage_test` was green through all three of these. Every one was a correct domain value that the presentation layer either re-derived Euclidean-ly (`labelText`), refused to draw (`isDefined`), or drew two pixels wide (the scale). When a milestone changes what values *mean*, audit every place a value is read for display, not only where it is computed.
- **`isDefined` is a question about ink, and `circle != null` is a question about Euclid.** Any predicate phrased in terms of the affine view (`circle`, `line`, `position`) is a Euclidean question wearing a general name. The narrow fallback matters: `isParameterized`, not `isDrawable` — the latter admits line pairs and would have started painting degenerate Euclidean circles and moved the goldens.
- **When a change moves goldens, ask what the goldens knew.** Raising `maxScale` looked like a nuisance and was in fact the discovery that fit and zoom had been sharing a limit that meant two different things.
- The triangle-angle-sum test written in Phase 126 passed throughout, because it reads `GeoAngle.measure` directly. It was right and it was not enough: nothing tested the string the user actually reads.
- **"Drawn in the right shape" and "visible" are different tests, and only one of them was written.** The painter test pins that the wash path contains a point outside the disc and excludes one inside; it was green through a wash nobody could see. Anything whose whole job is to be *noticed* needs a test that rasterizes and measures, not one that inspects geometry.
- **A green suite is not evidence about the renderer, and it took a user with a screenshot to establish that.** Four defects in this milestone were correct values that never reached the screen; this one was a correct *value*, a correct *shape*, and a renderer that drew neither. `test/web/` exists so the next one is caught by CI.
- **Read a bug report as a specification.** "The same colour unless I zoom enough to have part of the circumference off screen" names the exact discriminator — containment — and I twice reinterpreted it into something I already believed (too-faint alpha). The condition attached to a report is usually the most informative part of it.
- **When a rendering primitive has a platform-dependent implementation, prefer the formulation that does not use it.** An even-odd fill is not a workaround for the difference op; it is the more direct statement of "a fill with a hole", and it happens to have no boolean step to get wrong.
- **One opacity cannot serve two themes that start from opposite ends.** A tint over white and a lift over near-black are different quantities; deriving both from one alpha guarantees at least one of them is wrong.

## Session 130 (V2 Session 32) — 2026-08-18

**Done — Phase 126 (M-CK3) complete on `phase-126-ck-ui`, five commits.** The first phase of the milestone the user can *see*: three phases of kernel work were reachable only from tests, and this one opens the door. Suite **2500** green (from 2471), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, perf gate PASS at 1% / 7% of budget with unchanged checksums, `flutter build web --wasm` compiles.

- **The order was the design: the tool layer first, the codec's refusal last.** The refusal had been standing in for threading that was missing, so deleting it first would have shipped the failure it was protecting against. `ToolInput.absolute` is filled by the canvas from `Construction.kernel` and threaded through `resolvePoint`, `IntersectionTool`, `MidpointTool`, `MultiPointTool` and the macro tools; the `IntersectionPoint` factory takes it for the canonical remap alone.
- **Two findings from that threading, both pre-existing.** `coincidentExistingPoint` does not merely *answer* in the wrong geometry when defaulted — it perturbs every mutable root and recomputes the **whole construction**, so it leaves the entire document computed Euclidean, silently, until something else happens to recompute it. And three `recompute()` calls in `locus.dart` were still on the default: the static settle after a sweep, and both drive steps inside `_TracedSweep`, which had the absolute in hand the whole time.
- **The re-addressing premise is now demonstrated, not argued.** Two `FivePointConic`s through Euclidean-concyclic points that *miss*: two candidates under Euclidean, four under hyperbolic, and I and J sort **around** the complex pair rather than after it — so address 0 is a crossing in one geometry and a circular point in the other. A five-point conic is tier 1, so both conics are bit-identical in both geometries and the crossings do not move by an ulp. Only the numbering does.
- **`Construction.kernel` stopped being `final` without becoming a setter.** The only route is `switchKernel`, which switches, recomputes, re-points by chordal match and returns the report — there is no call that switches without re-addressing. The rule generalizes and is in PLAN: **when a mutation carries an obligation, make the obligation the only route rather than a rule the caller must remember.**
- **`SetGeometryCommand` records what the switch decided and replays it verbatim.** Nearest-match is not invertible: running it backwards can land a point where it never was, and a point with no position at the switch was never matched in either direction. What could not be matched is *reported*, not repaired.
- **The codec's refusal became support** — hyperbolic and elliptic documents load and round-trip with their geometry intact. The first guard stays: an *unknown* metric name is still refused. Version rule untouched, `test/fixtures/` still v1.
- **Disc-boundary rendering, and two deliberate silences.** `absoluteDisc` gives the hyperbolic unit circle with the outside washed, because that region is not part of the plane. Elliptic draws nothing because it *has* no real absolute and no edge; Euclidean because its absolute is ℓ∞, real but in no chart. Centre and radius come from the viewport, which is exact under rotation rather than approximate.
- **The mode as UI**: a geometry menu beside "Axes & grid", the switch through the command stack so it undoes like the edit it is, and the re-addressing report in a snack bar. Plus **the mini-tutorial the user asked for**, in the menu itself — four things to build that look different in each geometry, and the note that nothing in the toolbar changes; what changes is which theorems are true.
- **Gauss–Bonnet is a test now, not a promise.** A triangle's angle sum is exactly π under Euclidean, less under hyperbolic, more under elliptic, and the hyperbolic defect grows with the triangle and vanishes in the small limit. That is a *global* fact about three independent `VertexAngle`s, so an angle measure wrong by a scale, a sign, a supplement or a chart artefact cannot survive it — and it is the first claim the in-app guide makes, which is the right one to verify first.

**Next**
- Merge `phase-126-ck-ui`. That closes M-CK's four opened phases; the milestone's remaining work is the debt below rather than a numbered plan.
- **The load-time report for `repairedIntersections`** — long-standing, and now cheap: the geometry switch built exactly the reporting path the decoder needs, and the decoder still computes its repair list and shows nobody.
- Phase 125's coverage-gate debt: per-kind correctness beyond angle, and **`RotatedPoint`**, which is a gap rather than a refusal (a rotation about the disc centre *is* the Euclidean matrix).
- Still open, unchanged: the wasm browser smoke with the first deploy (the build compiles, the repo has no remote); no reply from the reporter on the mixed-tap-order re-test.

**Gotchas**
- **A whole-construction probe's parameter is not just what it answers in — it is what the document is left in.** `coincidentExistingPoint` restores the roots bit-exactly and then recomputes; a wrong absolute on that last pass rewrites every derived object in the document. Anything that recomputes more than its own subject needs the same scrutiny.
- **`branchIndex` divergence hides where taps cannot reach.** Real crossings are tier 1 of canonical order in every geometry, so a tap on a visible crossing addresses identically under either absolute — which is why the tool-layer hole was invisible. The divergence lives in the *complex* candidates, where I and J interleave, and it surfaces on reload. A test that only taps real crossings proves nothing here.
- **The re-addressing report is usually empty, and that is correct.** Most of a document is real transverse crossings that keep their indices. An unconditional "your points may have moved" warning would be noise and would train the user to dismiss the one time it matters.
- **Elliptic drawing nothing is a fact, not a TODO.** Twice while writing this it looked like a gap to fill. Elliptic space has no boundary and no unreachable region; anything drawn there is an edge the geometry does not have.
- The macro tools (square, rhombus, rectangle, parallelogram, right trapezium) now pass the absolute to their `IntersectionPoint`s, but they are still **Euclidean-shape** constructions built on parallels and perpendiculars, and `parallelThrough` refuses under a proper absolute. They degrade rather than generalize; that is expected and unaddressed.

## Session 129 (V2 Session 31) — 2026-08-18

**Done — Phase 125 (M-CK2) complete and merged.** The bulk of the milestone: 32 kinds, zero Euclidean behaviour change. Suite **2471** green (from 2438), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, perf gate PASS at 7% of budget with unchanged checksums. Merged as `1b76be8`.

- **`euclidean.dart` → `metric.dart`, every function taking an `Absolute`.** It was never only Euclidean; it was the Euclidean case of a family. Perpendicularity generalizes verbatim (`P ∨ poleOf(ℓ)`) and is now a test rather than the audit's claim; `parallelThrough` is **refused** under a proper absolute (zero triple) rather than approximated.
- **Midpoint and angle bisector are one construction, dually** (PLAN §"Two constructions, one algebra"). `M± = √⟨Q,Q⟩·P ± √⟨P,P⟩·Q`; under Euclidean `⟨P,P⟩ = w²`, so that reads `w_Q·P ± w_P·Q` — the affine midpoint **plus the point at infinity in direction `P−Q`**. The second root is the external midpoint, and its sitting at infinity is exactly why Euclidean looks like it has only one. The dual statement is `twoLineBisectorOf`, which had been computing the general formula with I and J substituted all along.
- **Circles split the way distance splits** (PLAN §"A circle follows its distance measure"). A circle is a level set of distance, so the Phase 124 split runs straight through it: under a proper absolute `K = ⟨R,R⟩(ΩC)(ΩC)ᵀ − ⟨C,R⟩²Ω`, the pencil through the absolute and the doubled polar of the centre, hence **bitangent to the absolute**. Under Euclidean that whole pencil collapses to multiples of ℓ∞, so `ck_circles.dart` is a **companion** to `circles.dart`, not a generalization of it. A centre *on* the absolute is not a degeneracy but a **horocycle**.
- **Three of the five named re-foundings just generalized**, because their definitions are constructions rather than formulas: the centroid is the medians' meet, the incentre the internal bisectors' meet, the inscribed circle centred there through the foot of a perpendicular. Worth keeping as a habit — check whether the Euclidean *formula* is a shortcut for a *construction* that generalizes before refusing a kind.
- **Refused with reasons at the site**: homothety and translation (a similarity that is not an isometry does not exist in CK geometry — Wallis), Apollonius/bifocal/focal conics and the radical axis (loci of distance ratios, sums and powers, which are logarithms here), the circle centre (a generalized eigenproblem for `{K, Ω}`), `Arc`/`Sector` extents and segment-ratio points (chart parameterizations).
- **`ProjTransform.harmonicHomology`** added, and it reproduces the shipped Euclidean reflection matrix **entry for entry** — the audit's claim that reflection *is* the homology about (axis, its pole), checked rather than asserted.
- **The circular-point filter is a fact about the Euclidean absolute**, so `intersectionCandidates` became absolute-aware and was threaded through every call site **at once**: `branchIndex` addresses the *filtered* order, so a caller left on the default would land its points in a different address space — the Phase 120c failure from a new direction.
- **The `recompute` default is kept, reversing Phase 124's schedule on purpose.** The Phase 121 precedent is about a kind silently *inheriting* a wrong value, which cannot happen when `Construction` supplies the parameter at every production call site. The remaining risk is a kind whose `recompute` ignores the parameter, and a signature cannot catch that — only behaviour can. `ck_kind_coverage_test` does, and it is a strictly stronger gate at ~140 fewer test call sites of churn.

**Next**
- **Phase 126** — the first phase the user can *see*: the kernel carried into the tool layer (owed before anything else, per the note left in `point_resolution.dart`), the geometry switch as a **re-addressing command**, the codec's refusal lifted, disc-boundary rendering, and the mode as UI.

**Gotchas**
- **A behavioural gate caught a bug a signature never would, and it was mine.** `GeoAngle.measure` was written `_measure ?? _angle?.measure`, which falls back to the chart sweep when the CK angle is null — i.e. silently reports a Euclidean number under a proper absolute. That is precisely what `ck_kind_coverage_test` exists to catch, and it caught it on the same commit that introduced it. Fixed with an explicit `_euclidean` flag; **never write the CK value as a `??` over the Euclidean one.**
- **Every CK measure must remove a global phase first.** The constructions take a square root of a form that is negative inside the absolute and hand back real elements scaled by `i`, so a pairing against a real element comes out imaginary and the measure goes null. The normalization divides by the pivot's **unit phase**, not the pivot: dividing by the component rounded exact zeros to 2.7e-17 and **lost every right angle**.
- **Conjugacy is perpendicularity in every CK geometry**, and the check must come *after* the isotropy check. A vanishing pairing between two non-isotropic lines is a right angle whatever the self-pairings' signs — but ℓ∞ is in the dual form's kernel and pairs to zero against everything, so ordering it first calls every isotropic line perpendicular to everything.
- **The compass-circle formula needs the `⟨C,C⟩` factor** that cancels in `ckCircleThrough`. It cancels for a *rim point* (the level is `⟨C,R⟩²/⟨R,R⟩`) and not for a *distance pair* (`⟨C,C⟩⟨P,Q⟩²/(⟨P,P⟩⟨Q,Q⟩)`). A real bug, caught only by cross-checking `ckCompassCircleOf` against `ckCircleWithRadius`.
- **Test intuitions to distrust in the disc**: a line through the disc *centre* has the same perpendicular in every geometry (the poles differ by sign), so a "a perpendicular differs" test there passes vacuously; the hyperbolic midpoint of `(0,0)`–`(0.8,0)` sits at `0.5`, *further out* than the Euclidean `0.4`; and `tanh(20)` is exactly `1.0` in a double, so a radius test must stay under ~5.
- **A coverage gate is not a proof.** `ck_kind_coverage_test` shows each kind *responds* to the absolute, not that its response is right. Per-kind correctness is pinned only where a test says so. `RotatedPoint` is the one honest **gap** rather than a refusal — a rotation about the disc centre *is* the Euclidean matrix, both proper absolutes being rotation-invariant there.
- Unchanged and still open: the load-time report for `repairedIntersections`; the wasm browser smoke with the first deploy; no reply from the reporter on the mixed-tap-order re-test.

## Session 128 (V2 Session 30) — 2026-08-17

**Done — M-CK opened; Phases 123 and 124 complete and merged.** The kernel track closed at 122, so this session opened the Cayley–Klein milestone with its own PLAN section and took its first two phases. Both phases found their own premise to be partly wrong, and in both cases that was the deliverable.

- **Phase 123 (M-CK0) — the absolute, and the audit that scopes the milestone.**
- **`Absolute` carries both matrices, point conic and dual, given explicitly.** Not an optimization: the Euclidean absolute *as a point conic* is the doubled line at infinity, **rank 1**, and the adjugate of a rank-1 matrix vanishes identically — so the dual is not ill-conditioned to recover, it is unrecoverable. The dual is the point pair {I, J}, `diag(1,1,0)`, and it is the half the code actually reads: `normalDirectionOf`, `focalConicOf`'s `a²+b²` and `ProjTransform.reflection`'s `m22` are three independent arrivals at it. (PLAN said "rank-2 point conic" when the section was drafted; that conflated the two matrices and is corrected.)
- **The audit: 38 of 50 kinds are absolute-dependent, not the 25 the import census predicted.** 53 files, 50 concrete kinds, 3 abstract bases; Phase 121's "44" reconciles as these 50 minus the six measurement kinds, which carry no projective value. The census missed two whole routes — `proj_transform.dart` is a **fifth** metric kernel module that none of the four censused files import, and the six measurement kinds import nothing metric *because they measure in the chart directly*, which is the Phase 112 boundary working as designed and invisible to a grep.
- **Structural result: the metric is one operation, not a scattering.** Read as projective constructions rather than formulas, most of tiers 2–3 already *are* the CK construction with the Euclidean absolute substituted — `perpendicularThrough` joins `P` to the pole of `ℓ`; `ReflectedPoint` is the harmonic homology about (axis, its pole); `CentralReflectionPoint` about (centre, its polar), which is ℓ∞ here, which is why it currently reads as a homothety of −1. Five genuine re-foundings named for Phase 125.
- **`branchIndex` addressing is relative to the absolute** — the milestone's sharpest constraint, and not in the outline. The candidate list filters the circular points and canonical order keys on a centre, which is `poleOf(ℓ∞)`. Per document it is consistent and nothing breaks; but switching a *live* document's geometry re-addresses every intersection point, which is the Phase 120c defect if shipped as a display toggle. Assigned to 126, and it is why `Construction.kernel` is **final**.
- `DocumentKernel` moved to the domain layer; `encodeDocument` reads the kernel off the construction rather than taking it as a parameter.
- **Phase 124 (M-CK1) — measurement, and the half of it that is not a cross-ratio.**
- **Angle unifies; distance does not.** Measured before building: `cos θ = |⟨ℓ,m⟩*|/√(⟨ℓ,ℓ⟩*⟨m,m⟩*)` against the dual absolute is Laguerre's formula with the logs cleared, and under `diag(1,1,0)` it *is* the textbook Euclidean angle — agreement **7.4e-13** over 20k random line pairs. Distance has no such route: a line meets the doubled ℓ∞ in one point twice, a cross-ratio with a repeated point is identically 1, and its log is identically 0 for every pair at every constant. Algebraically `⟨P,Q⟩ = w₁w₂` — the Euclidean pairing has forgotten where the points are. This is the Cayley–Klein classification as a code constraint: Euclidean has **elliptic angle measure and parabolic distance**.
- So **angle substitutes and distance dispatches.** Both keep their exact chart route under Euclidean, for opposite reasons: for distance the chart is the only place the quantity exists; for angle the chart formula already *is* the CK one, so the specialization is exact and a right angle still reads exactly π/2. Phase 122's circle-fast-path rule in both cases.
- **The absolute now reaches every recompute.** 52 signatures changed; the ~150 call sites did not, the parameter being optional with a Euclidean default — migration scaffolding with a removal scheduled for the end of 125, exactly as the lift-from-affine defaults were. `Construction` passes `kernel.absolute` at all seven of its call sites, so the default is never a live fallback.
- **`GeoAngle` gained `measure` beside `angle`**: a marker is not a measure. The arc is still drawn between the same two chords while the number is the CK angle. Same rule as a measurement's anchor.
- **Length, area and slope go undefined under a proper absolute** rather than reporting a Euclidean number — arc length and area are *integrals* (hyperbolic circumference `2π·sinh r`; a hyperbolic triangle's area is its angular defect), and slope is a chart ratio against distinguished axes.
- Suite **2438** green (from 2397), analyze clean, 32 goldens byte-identical, perf gate PASS at 1% / 7% of budget with unchanged checksums.

**Next**
- **Phase 125** — the tier-2/3 substitutions in the audit's order: the verbatim pole/polar generalizations first, then the five genuine re-foundings (`Incenter`/`InscribedCircle`, `RadicalAxisLine`, `CircleCenter`, `FixedRadiusCircle`'s radius param, `BifocalConic`), then the refusals made real in the tools and the inspector. Remove the `recompute` default at its end.
- **Phase 126** is the first phase the user can *see*: the mode as UI, disc rendering, and the geometry switch as a re-addressing command.
- The user has flagged that the milestone's payoff is three phases from its start and asked whether anything is visible yet (no, by design). An option was offered and not taken up: bring a crude 126 forward so there is something to play with while 124/125 fill in underneath. Worth re-offering if 125 runs long.
- Still open, unchanged: the load-time validation/report pass for duplicate intersection addresses (the decoder computes `repairedIntersections` and **nothing displays it**); the wasm browser smoke with the first deploy; no reply from the reporter on the mixed-tap-order re-test.

**Gotchas**
- **An import census undercounts a cross-cutting dependency, and quietly.** It said 25 of 52; the answer was 38 of 50. Both misses were structural rather than careless — a fifth kernel module, and a tier that reaches the metric by *not* importing anything. When the question is "what depends on X", grep for the operations, not the imports.
- **Which lines are isotropic is a fact about the geometry, and it bites in tests.** `3x − 4y + 5 = 0` is an ordinary Euclidean line and is tangent to the hyperbolic absolute (distance from the origin exactly 5/5 = 1), so it has no hyperbolic angle to anything. A test picked it for its integer coefficients and failed; it is now a case.
- **Two test failures this session were wrong expectations, not wrong code** (`acos(3/5)` for `acos(4/5)`; a "right angle" built from `(3,4)` and `(0,5)`). Both looked like kernel bugs for a moment. Check the expected value by hand before suspecting a formula that has a 20k-pair agreement test behind it.
- The `recompute` default means a kind's **constructor** (`recompute()` with no argument) computes Euclidean regardless of the document. Harmless because `Construction.add` immediately recomputes with `kernel.absolute` — but it is a real ordering dependency, and it goes away with the default in 125.
- Nothing non-Euclidean is reachable from the app: the codec still refuses both other metrics, so every CK path is exercised only from tests. That is deliberate for 123–125 and ends at 126.

## Session 127 (V2 Session 29) — 2026-08-17

**Done — Phase 121 merged, Phase 122 complete** on `phase-122-perf-hardening`, three commits. The phase's own premise turned out to be wrong, and finding that out was most of the value: it was written as "tracing inner loop onto SoA `Float64List`", and the first thing measured said that path is **1.4%** of a traced recompute.

- **Merged `phase-121-kernel-deletion` into main** after re-verifying it on this tree (analyze clean, 2392 green), as session 126 said to.
- **Measured before rewriting.** On a traced `IntersectionPoint.recompute` (VM), `TracedBranch.follow` — the entire nearest-root match, motion and separation bookkeeping — is 43 ns against the recompute's 3044; `checkpoint` 11 ns, `restore` 6. Everything else is `intersectionCandidates`. So the phase's budget went where the time was instead.
- **Two circles were solving a cubic they never needed.** `intersectionCandidates` on two circles cost 2875 ns against line∩conic's 363 — 8× — because it ran the general four-root pencil (balance → cubic → root clustering → member scoring → adjugate split) and then discarded I and J, which it had just recovered *numerically* and which are known exactly. But a conic is a circle exactly when `xy = 0`, `xx = yy`, and for two of those `b.xx·A − a.xx·B` kills the quadratic block outright, leaving `w·(2Dx + 2Ey + Fw)` — the pencil's degenerate member in closed form, ℓ∞ together with the radical axis. Steps 2–4 collapse to two subtractions. The Newton polish goes with them (it cleans up a member found at an inexact root; there is none). The centroid balance **stays**, free here since a circle's centre is `(−xw/xx, −yw/xx)`, and measured load-bearing: unbalanced at 1e6 offset the answer is 2e-5 out against the balanced form's 1.3e-10.
- **Then canonical ordering was half of what remained** — 505 ns of 1080 — and mostly a tie-break nobody reaches. Not the centre recovery (handing those in is worth 56 ns); it was building four `_OrderKey`s eagerly, each with its lexicographic list and a `normalized` behind it, when a circle pair gives two real points and then I and J, where tier and primary always decide. Lazy now. `intersectConicConic` on two circles: **2676 → 947 ns**.
- **Measured end to end** on `locus-miss-2.json` (a circle∩circle chain): **5.85 → 3.02 µs/solve** VM, 8.53 → 3.47 AOT, 8.28 → 3.92 dart2js, 8.65 → 3.69 dart2wasm; the sweep 4.30 → 1.75 ms AOT. **Solve counts identical** (504), so the walk made the same decisions, and `apatitos-topos.rgl` — line∩circle throughout — is unchanged at 2.04 µs, the control that says the win is where it was claimed.
- **The drag budget is a gate now, not a printed opinion**: `tracing_bench.dart` throws when a frame exceeds `dragFrameBudgetMs`. Thrown rather than `exit`ed — `dart:io` would cost that file its js and wasm targets, and an uncaught error is non-zero on all four. Verified by temporarily setting an impossible budget.
- **Benchmark suite in CI**, informational: `benchmark/run_ci.sh` (VM-only, dependency-free, ~1 min) behind `.github/workflows/benchmarks.yml`, on push to main plus dispatch, writing to the step summary. Deliberately non-failing — a shared runner's absolute timings are not what an 8 ms budget is about.
- **Compile target settled: dart2wasm, deploy switched.** wasm wins raw complex arithmetic 1.4–1.7× and is a wash on the whole engine (it takes the documents and the gate, and *loses* `intersectionCandidates` on two circles, 2345 vs 2060 ns). What settles it is the payload: `--wasm` emits **both**, so a WasmGC browser fetches 2.4 MB of wasm instead of 2.8 MB of js while older browsers fall back to exactly what they get today.
- **SoA closed as not worth doing** — measured, not reversed. Phase 101's 1.30–1.78× on the arithmetic is real; applied to a slot that is 3–6% of a recompute it is a ceiling of 1–2.5% of a frame, under the gate's own noise. Unboxing the *kernel* (the 85–97%) is a different, much larger question, left unopened while nothing is bottlenecked.
- New coverage: I and J returning at exactly `w = 0` (the discriminator that the short route is *taken* — the general one leaves them at `|w| ~ 1e-33`); both routes agreeing index for index over 400 random pairs, the general one reached by nudging a conic 1e-30 off the circle shape, with the real and conjugate regimes both asserted reached; the balance load-bearing at 1e6; complex carriers incident on both conics; concentric circles meeting only at I and J. Suite **2397 green**, 32 goldens byte-identical, analyze clean, both web builds compile, perf gate PASS on all four targets at 1–2% / 7–11% of budget with identical checksums. TODO rotated: Phase 120c to `docs/archive/`.

**Next**
- Merge `phase-122-perf-hardening` — the kernel track's last phase, so merging it closes Phases 100–122.
- Then the milestones are what is left: **M-CK** (Cayley–Klein, unlocked since 121), **M-P** (prover, startable since 112), **M-3D** (last). None has a numbered phase yet; opening one means a PLAN section first.
- Still open from the 120c arc: a **load-time validation/report pass** so a document carrying duplicate intersection addresses tells the user. Worth a phase number rather than prose.
- Still open with the reporter: they were asked to re-test the recorded mixed-tap-order sequence. No reply recorded, two sessions on.
- `flutter build web --wasm` compiles but **has never run in a browser** — the repo still has no remote, so the deploy workflow has never fired. That smoke belongs with the first deploy.

**Gotchas**
- **Measure which *function* holds the time before deciding which *representation* to use.** The phase carried a well-founded prior from Phase 101 and it was pointed at 1.4% of the cost. The prior was not wrong about arithmetic; it could not know how little of a frame the arithmetic-heavy slot would be next to the graph recompute around it. The 2.8× came from deleting an algorithm, and it was invisible until something asked where the time was.
- **A benchmark can pass a gate without exercising what the gate is about.** `tracing_bench.dart` runs at 1.00 trials/frame with 0.00 rejected — it accepts every trial, so the walk's inner loop barely runs. It is a fine budget check and a poor profile; that is now written at the top of the file, pointing at `chain_solve_bench.dart` and `locus_docs_bench.dart` for the other two questions.
- **`dart:io` in `benchmark/` silently costs a file its js and wasm targets.** Two of the four run under node. Use a thrown error for a non-zero exit.
- **The circle dispatch test is exact (`xy == 0 && xx == yy`) on purpose.** Every constructor in `circles.dart` emits `xx` and `yy` as the same expression so it holds bitwise; a `FivePointConic` through five concyclic points misses it by rounding and takes the general route, which is correct and only slower. Do not "improve" it into a tolerance — a tolerance is how a non-circle gets misrouted.
- **Watch for a vacuous max when a comparison list can be empty.** An early accuracy measurement read "the pencil is exact at 1e9 offset" when the pencil had in fact returned *no points* and the max ran over an empty list. Both routes give up there, for the same reason: `ww ≈ 2e18` has swallowed the radius in the conic's own entries.
- The `_OrderKey` tie-break is lazy now but the *rule* is untouched. `_canonicalOrder` is where every route's ordering is decided, and `branchIndex` addresses its output — keep it that way; the circle route was integrated by handing it a centre, never by ordering its own points.

## Session 126 (V2 Session 28) — 2026-08-17

**Done — Phase 121 complete**, on `phase-121-kernel-deletion`, eight commits. Every item on the checklist, plus two findings the work turned up. The through-line: four of the seven items turned out to be **already true in code**, and the honest deliverable for those was not a diff but a measurement, a statement at the declaration site, and a gate that keeps it true.

- **One detour half-plane for every kind of pass.** Loci detoured through a rule odd in the drive direction (`detourOrientation1D`) while drags took a constant; the constant is now `detourHalfPlane` (renamed from `dragDetourOrientation`, no longer drag-only) and the odd rule is deleted. **Fixture diffs nil, bitwise**, measured *before* switching: a census over all four locus documents shows only `apatitos-topos.rgl` detours at all — twice, both on *forward* legs, which is exactly where the two rules disagreed — and its samples are identical under the odd rule, under `+1` and under `−1`. The reason generalizes, so that is what got recorded: an arc encircles its one estimated singularity by a half-turn whichever side it passes, and a half-turn either way exchanges the two sheets of a simple branch point, so the half-plane can only matter when an arc encloses more than one collision or one of higher order — where the arc is mis-planned and neither rule is right. New coverage pins that the Phase 117b rig *detours at all* (two arcs, no folds); it and apatitos are the whole corpus's detour coverage.
- **The index-flip guard works at any candidate count.** `locus.dart` read the un-matched root as `1 - index` and skipped itself on anything but a two-candidate pair, so a conic∩conic chain member lost the guard rather than crashing. Widened into `trace_acceptance.dart` as `relabelIsBenign` with its own tests: a flip is benign exactly when every root the slot did *not* follow pairs with a distinct other root within the same motion allowance. Nearest-match is **exact**, not merely greedy, because the cap is at most half the minimum pairwise separation; at two candidates it reduces to the old arithmetic exactly.
- **The old affine kernel left `lib/`** — and not just `intersections.dart`, the phase's named target. `angle_bisector.dart`, `circle_relations.dart`, `harmonic.dart` and `tangents.dart` were in the same state, no `lib/` importer since Phase 110 superseded each. All five **moved** to `test/v1_oracle/` rather than deleted, because deleting them deletes the *evidence* that the projective kernels still agree with V1 on real transverse cases — the contract `branchIndex` addresses, permanent because v1 documents are. Six test files consume them as oracles. Living under `test/` turns the standing rule from a grep gate into the directory layout.
- **`LineEq`/`CircleEq` demoted to view structs** — already true (nothing constructs either outside `lib/domain/`, and inside it only through `toLineEq`/`toCircleEq` or behind a guard), so the work was saying it in both headers and CLAUDE.md, whose "legacy affine math (frozen; shrinking)" row had stopped describing what is left. One real exception fixed: `InscribedCircle` passed `inradius(...)!` into `CircleEq` on the strength of two helpers sharing a collinearity test.
- **The bridge's lift-from-affine defaults are abstract.** All 44 kinds already implemented `projPoint`/`projLine`/`conic` (eight only through a migrated intermediate base, which is why a per-file grep undercounts), as did every test stub. Worth doing rather than leaving harmless: the default *inverts* after the migration, from a correct fallback into a silent one that would let a new kind forget its homogeneous state.
- **`point_coincidence.dart` needed no change, and now says why.** Two of its consequences look like oversights and are not: an off-chart point has no position and is never merged (two pairs of parallels meeting at the *same* projective point stay two points — the conservative branch this function takes everywhere), and the tolerance stays in world units because it screens "one dot or two". Both pinned by a test.
- **One degeneracy convention, stated and gated.** Audited across all 44 kinds and found uniform: null only when a parent's projective value is null or the computed value is the zero triple. Stated on `GeoObject` with its three sanctioned exceptions named. Gating it per kind would need 44 hand-built degeneracies; **collapsing every free point in the kitchen-sink corpora onto one position produces all of them at once**, and 29 views survive to be checked — which is itself the convention showing, a degeneracy being mostly a value rather than an absence.
- **PLAN §"Migration strategy" is now §"Architecture"** — same five points, rewritten as what the migration arrived at, rejected alternatives kept. CLAUDE.md's invariants follow; "migrated kind"/"unmigrated objects" retired.
- **Two literal NUL bytes found and escaped**, one in `construction.dart` (the Phase 120c per-pair adoption key) and — after documenting the first — one I wrote into `docs/TODO.md` describing it. Dart and Markdown both read them fine; `file(1)` calls the file "data" and grep/ripgrep skip it **silently**, so every search in the largest domain file came back empty rather than wrong. Found by grepping for the drag side's detour orientation and getting nothing.
- Suite **2392 green**, 32 goldens byte-identical, analyze clean, `flutter build web` compiles, perf gate PASS on VM/AOT/dart2js/dart2wasm (1–2% of budget, 7–11% with the locus rig), checksums identical. TODO rotated: Phases 115–120b to `docs/archive/`.

**Next**
- Merge `phase-121-kernel-deletion`; then **Phase 122** — the last kernel-track phase: tracing inner loop on SoA `Float64List` (no boxed `Complex` in the hot loop), benchmark suite as an informational CI job, the final dart2js-vs-dart2wasm decision, and the drag budget re-measured as the standing gate.
- Still open from the 120c arc: a **load-time validation/report pass** so a document carrying duplicate intersection addresses tells the user. Worth opening as a numbered phase rather than carrying it in prose.
- Still open with the reporter: they were asked to re-test the recorded mixed-tap-order sequence. No reply recorded.

**Gotchas**
- **A literal control byte makes a file invisible to search, not broken** — compiler happy, tests green, every `grep`/`rg` silently empty. If a search for a symbol you can *see* in a file returns nothing, run `file(1)` on it before doubting the symbol. Both instances this session were written by hand into a string that wanted a NUL; use `\u0000` / `\x00`.
- **Four of seven Phase 121 items were already done.** The temptation was to tick them; the value was in *proving* it (the detour census, the 44-kind collapse, the parallels-at-infinity test) and stating it where the next reader will hit it. A checklist item that turns out to need no code is not a no-op, it is an unwritten invariant.
- **`test/v1_oracle/` is a deliberate exception to "tests mirror source layout"** — it is not source any more. Do not move things in there because they look old: the test is "no `lib/` consumer *and* something in `test/` compares against it". Do not extend or "fix" what is in there; its value is being the build that shipped.
- `relabelIsBenign`'s exactness rests on `cap ≤ separation/2`. A looser cap makes nearest-match ambiguous and would need a real assignment algorithm; the call site derives the cap from `_checkpoints[i]!.separation / 2`, so don't loosen it there.
- The locus walk still differs from the drag walk in one recorded way, and it is not the half-plane: a locus arc is planned in the leg's *distance travelled* (always increasing) and mapped to the drive variable by `dir`, so the physical side a run detours through now flips with walk direction. Nothing observable depends on it — see the census above — but keep the two parameter spaces straight when reading `_traceArc`.
- The degeneracy-collapse gate asserts `checked > 20` against 29 today. If a future kind legitimately drops that count, don't just lower the bound — the point of the guard is that the loop never goes vacuous.

## Session 125 (V2 Session 27) — 2026-08-17

**Done**
- The reporter said the ellipse issue still stood and **sent a screen recording** (`~/Movies/2026-08-17_17-58-54.mkv`, 55 s). It settled in one pass what three rounds of rig-building had not: frames pulled at 1 fps, then 3 fps across the failure, show two bifocal ellipses with four crossings, points G/H/I/J on them, a focus dragged — and at 50.3–50.8 s the two *lower* crossings go complex and H and I vanish, then at 51.3 s **come back stacked on one side**, the other crossing vacant. Exactly the report, with the mechanism visible: a partial degeneracy, two of four crossings, not the whole pair separating.
- Reproducing it still took work, and the failures are worth recording because each killed a plausible theory: 1176 loop drags (asymmetric excursions), 770 two-gesture round trips (release *while* the points are undefined, which is what the reporter describes), coarse gestures down to **one frame per drag** — every one of which fired the static bail, 770/770 — and a candidate-list histogram proving the `min(branchIndex, len-1)` clamp never bites for conic∩conic (always 4). **Zero violations in all of them.**
- What broke it on the first try was **mixing tap order**: three crossings tapped one way round, the fourth the other. **1237 of 3078.** All-same-order: zero. The signature was unmistakable — a point born at a distinct crossing with `branchIndex` 1, then after one drag holding index 0 and sitting exactly on top of another point.
- **Root cause: `branchIndex` is only meaningful relative to a parent order, and the two orders are not a fixed permutation of each other.** The ordering key is the directed centre line `curve1 → curve2`; reversing it flips the **real-finite tier only**, leaving points at infinity and non-real ones alone. So how the two numberings correspond depends on how many candidates are currently real — which changes as the figure moves. Two points on opposite orderings are two incompatible address spaces, and every guard added last session (tool dedup, atomic adoption, decoder repair) compares within one. Last session'"'"s cross-order chordal match in the tool patched the *creation* route only; adoption was still free to walk them together.
- **Fix: `IntersectionPoint` stores its pair in canonical order (object id ascending) whichever way round the caller names it**, renumbering the requested branch onto that order by chordal match at construction. The crossing the caller asked for is the crossing it tracks; only the address changes. This is a modelling correction, not a patch — the *unordered* pair is the intersection'"'"s identity, and tap order never meant anything. Every consumer (`construction.dart` adoption, `trace_acceptance.dart`, `locus.dart`, the codec, the tool) reads `curve1, curve2` as a pair and became correct for free; the tool'"'"s cross-order machinery and the codec'"'"s reference-ordering machinery both **deleted as dead**, so this landed smaller than the patch it replaces.
- **No format change.** No build writes a non-canonical pair, so finding one in a file *is* the marker that it predates this — the version field is not needed to tell them apart, and all six fixtures stay v1. An old file'"'"s reversed pair is canonicalized on load, and if that lands two points on one crossing the existing decoder repair separates them.
- Behaviour change, deliberate: branch **labels** mirror for pairs that used to be stored reversed. Positions do not. Six expectations record it (the Cinderella rigs at engine and session level, the v1 corpus'"'"s parents-order assertion, the circle-first tap test, a locus flip-restoration assertion rewritten to assert *restored* rather than a literal).
- Regression test is the recording'"'"s own sequence: tap three crossings in mixed tap orders, drag away until they vanish, drag back, tap the vacancy, drag again — asserting no two points share a crossing and no two share a branch. Plus canonical storage and renumbering on a reversed tap. **Both fail on the pre-fix tree** (checked by stashing the two lib files).
- Suite **2378 green**, 32 goldens byte-identical, analyze clean, perf gate PASS on VM, dart2js and dart2wasm (2% / 10% of the 8 ms budget, checksums identical).

- **Post-merge tidy, on the reporter's asks.** The tangent and polar tools were labelled "point and circle" in both the toolbar and the shortcut table; both are polar constructions on the parent's conic matrix and have never needed a circular parent, so the labels now say "point and conic" — with the coverage that makes it a checked claim rather than a promise (a genuine ellipse: both tangents through the pole meeting it in a double root at the chord of contact, an interior pole with complex tangents and a real polar, the centre polarizing to the line at infinity). Radical axis deliberately keeps "two circles": it is the pencil member whose quadratic block cancels, which needs the parents to share the circular points.
- **`dart format` adopted, in its own commit.** Sessions 121 and 124 banned it because the SDK formatter emits the tall style (Dart 3.7) while the tree was last formatted short, so any edit dragged in unrelated reflow. That made the tree permanently unformattable, which is the worse state. Landed alone: 135 files, and behaviour preservation *verified* rather than assumed — each changed file diffed against its pre-format text with all whitespace stripped, giving 41 whitespace-only and 94 whitespace-plus-trailing-comma, and **zero** differing by any other token. Generated `*.g.dart` was already tall style, so regeneration will not fight it. The policy now lives in CLAUDE.md rather than in a STATUS note nobody reads.
- The reporter also asked whether intersection names "rolling" across repeated drags is expected. It is, and it is now pinned: an identical out-and-back gesture trades exactly the two crossings that go complex and the next trip trades them back (two states, strictly alternating), while *varying* the excursion realizes different elements of the monodromy group — which is what reads on screen as rolling. Across forty varied round trips all four names survive, none doubles, no two share a branch.

**Next**
- Merge `phase-120c-conic-defects` — the branch now carries all three defects plus this root cause, and has been offered three times.
- Ask the reporter to re-test the recorded sequence specifically: tap the four crossings in *different* orders (e1 first for some, e2 first for others), which is what the recording did and what every previous rig had not.
- Phase 121 as planned, plus the three items this arc added: unify the locus detour orientation with the drag constant (fixture diffs reviewed deliberately), widen `locus.dart`'"'"s two-candidate index-flip guard, and consider a load-time validation/report pass.

**Gotchas**
- **Ask for a recording.** Three rounds of rig-building could not reproduce this; 55 seconds of video located it in one pass, because it showed the *partial* degeneracy (two of four crossings) and, implicitly, that the four points had not all been built the same way. For any "still broken" report where the rig passes, a screen recording is the cheapest next move — `ffmpeg -vf fps=N` plus contact sheets reads it quickly.
- **A guard that compares indices is only as good as the address space they live in.** Last session closed three mutation routes and still missed this, because all three compared `branchIndex` within one parent order. Normalizing the *representation* is what made those guards total — and it deleted more code than it added.
- **Note which theories a fuzz kills, not just that it passed.** The bail path, the clamp path, the release-while-undefined path and the asymmetric-loop path were all eliminated by measurement here. Without recording that, a future session re-runs them.
- The `assert` on `IntersectionPoint.canonical` is load-bearing: it is what caught the scripted test double building a non-canonical pair. Keep it.
- **The `dart format` ban is lifted** (see CLAUDE.md §Conventions). Do not re-impose it citing sessions 121 or 124: their reason was a pending short→tall migration, which has now landed. Keep formatting out of behaviour commits, that is all.
- `canonicalPairOrder` compares **ids**, which are stable across save/load and independent of tap order. It does not matter which order is canonical, only that every point on a pair agrees; do not "improve" it into something geometric, which would move as the figure moves — the exact bug this fixes.

## Session 124 (V2 Session 26) — 2026-08-17

**Done**
- The reporter re-tested session 123 and **two of the three were still broken in the app**. Both had the same root cause in kind: the fix had been verified at the wrong level.
- **Issue 2, the real culprit: a *third* `0..1` bound, on the commit path.** `Construction.setIntersectionBranch` — the primitive a *committed* re-pointing goes through — carried its own `must be 0 or 1`. So a traced drag on two conics adopting index 2 or 3 threw out of `MoveFreePointCommand.apply`, the gesture's re-pointing was discarded, and the four crossings went on merging exactly as reported. Session 123's regression tests drove `Construction.recomputeAlongPath` directly: they exercised the engine and never once went through the command. Fixed, and **the missing level now has coverage** — `DragSession.start / update / end / apply` over the reported document, asserting four distinct branches on four distinct roots, plus undo restoring the pre-drag indices. An existing test asserted `setIntersectionBranch(2)` *throws*; it was pinning the bug and is updated.
- Every bound on `branchIndex` now derives from `maxBranchCount`, with the three past failures recorded on the constant: the constructor threw on the third crossing (120b), adoption collapsed branches silently, the commit primitive threw out of the command (both 120c). Three hand-written literals, three different failure modes.
- **Issue 3: the convention was wrong, not just its mechanism.** Session 123 built *alternation* — each point remembering its last detour half-plane and negating it — which is a way of keeping **reversal-identity** (a round trip restores every branch) without reading the drag direction. Asked directly, the reporter wanted the opposite: **honest monodromy**, Cinderella's behaviour, where dragging two curves apart through a tangency and back *trades* the two crossings and doing it again trades them back.
- That is the simpler rule, and it is now a **constant** (`dragDetourOrientation`). A return pass parameterizes the reverse path, so its `Im s > 0` is the outward pass's `Im t < 0`; a constant half-plane therefore sends the outward leg over the branch point and the return leg under it, closing a loop — and a loop around a branch point is not the identity. `detourOrientation` is deleted, and the alternation machinery (`IntersectionPoint.lastDetourOrientation`, `_alternatingOrientation`) went with it, so the engine came out smaller than it went in.
- **The seam defect is closed either way**, which is worth separating from the convention change: the old rule read the drag direction, any ±1 rule on directions has a seam, and that one's lay on the horizontal axis (sign of `dy`, `dx` only when `dy` was exactly zero) — where "drag two equal circles onto each other" lives. A constant reads nothing about the gesture. Measured through the drag session as the app runs it: 0 round trips → lower crossing, 1 → upper, 2 → lower, 3 → upper, 4 → lower, and **every one of 30 (jitter × seed) combinations per trip count agrees**, at 0, 0.05 and 1 px of jitter and at coarse frames.
- Tests flipped from identity to monodromy: the Phase 115 canonical there-and-back, the 116b parameter-drive twin, and session 123's own group. Locus sweeps keep `detourOrientation1D` deliberately — a one-directional scan has no round trip to be honest about, and its sheet choices are pinned by the 117b fixtures; unifying is a Phase 121 item now written down as such.
- Suite **2372 green**, 32 goldens byte-identical, analyze clean, perf gate PASS (1% / 7% of budget). Decisions re-pinned in PLAN §"A round trip is honest: the detour half-plane is constant".

- **Then the reporter said the ellipse issue was still standing — and the decisive evidence was in their file all along.** `ellipse-intersection-issue.rgl` holds six intersection points on one conic pair, and **two of them are exact duplicates**: same ordered parents, same `branchIndex` (`a70e/e277#1` twice, `e277/a70e#1` twice). Objects with the same pair and the same branch *are* the same intersection by construction, so they load at the same position and no engine fix can ever separate them — the file is permanently corrupt, written by the pre-fix build. Two visible points on one side, the crossings they should occupy empty: exactly the report. A repaired copy (`-repaired.rgl`, the two duplicates dropped, no dependents) loads with four points, four distinct keys, four distinct positions.
- Fresh constructions were already clean, and that is now measured rather than assumed: a **300-seed gesture fuzz** — random grabs of any free point *or* either ellipse, wandering far enough to separate the pair, eight gestures per seed, through `DragSession` and its command — found **zero** violations of "four distinct branch addresses, no two visible points coincident".
- **But the fuzz missed a real gap the file itself pointed at**: three points sat on `e277/a70e` and three on `a70e/e277`. `branchIndex` addresses the canonical order of `intersectionCandidates(curve1, curve2)`, which is *not* the reversed pair's order, so the same crossing carries a different index depending on which curve the user tapped first. The dedup's structural arm compared ordered parents and could not see across that; the positional arm could, but only while the crossing was real. So with the crossings complex, a reversed-order tap built a fresh point on an occupied branch — the accumulation route that survived the last round. Reversed candidates are now matched to this pair's by nearest **chordal** distance, which is defined on complex candidates and so speaks in exactly the case proximity cannot.

- **Third report: fresh ellipses, vertical foci, dragging a focus out and back — and "the same does not happen with circles".** That last clause is the useful one: circles are conic∩conic with I/J filtered, so two candidates; ellipses are four. Still **not reproducible headlessly** — a 300-seed gesture fuzz (any free point or either ellipse, eight gestures each, through the session and its command) and 21 targeted focus drags on *axis-aligned four-crossing* pairs both came back with zero violations of "four distinct branch addresses, no two visible points coincident". Axis-aligned pairs were worth testing specifically: vertical foci means a symmetric configuration, which is where a pencil solver would be expected to fail. It does not.
- So the response was to stop hunting the route and **close the invariant at every route**, which is what the reporter actually asked for ("once and for all"). `(curve1, curve2, branchIndex)` identifies an intersection, so two points sharing it are the same point, permanently and silently. Now defended at creation (the tool, across parent order), at adoption (atomic per pair), and at load (the decoder separates and reports). Pinned in PLAN §"Two intersection points may never share a branch".
- **The reported file cannot be fully repaired and that is not a bug**: it holds *six* points for four crossings. Re-pointing cannot fit six into four, so two stay coincident; the surplus has to be deleted. `ellipse-intersection-issue-repaired.rgl` (written this session) does exactly that and loads clean.

**Next**
- **Ask the reporter for a freshly saved `.rgl` of the failing state, and for the commit they are running.** Three rounds have now gone by with the engine passing every rig; the file settled the last round in thirty seconds and would settle this one. The branch is still unmerged, so a build from `main` — or from before the `setIntersectionBranch` fix — would explain everything.
- Merge `phase-120c-conic-defects`; then Phase 121. Its detour-orientation item is now **settled** rather than deferred; two smaller items replaced it (unify the locus orientation with the fixture diffs reviewed deliberately, and widen `locus.dart`'s two-candidate index-flip guard).
- Worth considering there: nothing stops a *document* from carrying two intersection points with the same pair and branch. The codec faithfully reproduces whatever it is given, which is right, but the app has no way to tell a user their file holds a duplicate, and no way to repair one. A validation pass on load — report, don't silently rewrite — would have turned this session's three-round diagnosis into one glance.

**Gotchas**
- **Verify a drag fix at session level, not engine level.** This is the session's real lesson and it cost a round trip with the reporter. `Construction.recomputeAlongPath` is not what the app calls — `DragSession` is, and the gesture ends in a *command*, which is a second place the same invariant is enforced and can be enforced differently. A green engine test proves less than it looks like it proves.
- **Ask what an observable should be before engineering it.** "Swap directions each time" was implemented as swapping the detour's half-plane, and meant swapping the *points*. The two are opposites in effect, and a week of alternation machinery was the cost. When a request names a behaviour that could be internal or user-visible, pin down which.
- The convention is now recorded in two places that must agree: `singularity.dart`'s library doc and PLAN §"A round trip is honest". Both explain reversal-identity as the rejected alternative, because it is a legitimate design that a future reader may otherwise "restore" as an obvious improvement.
- Loci and drags now use *different* detour conventions. Nothing is wrong today (a locus has no round trip), but it is a real non-uniformity and the choice does change which sheet a locus run continues onto past a crossing — the 117b fixtures pin the current answers, so changing it is not a drive-by.
- `dragDetourOrientation`'s *value* is arbitrary; that it is constant is not. Don't "improve" it into a function of anything.
- **Check the reporter's document for pre-existing corruption before hunting the engine.** Two rounds went into an engine that was, by the end, behaving correctly on every rig — while the file itself carried two exact duplicate objects that no fix could undo. `decodeDocument` plus a histogram of `(curve1, curve2, branchIndex)` is thirty seconds and should be the *first* move on any "still broken" report about intersections.
- **Guard the invariant, not the route.** Three rounds went into finding *how* duplicates were being created. The durable answer was to make the state unrepresentable at every mutation route, which took less work than any one of the hunts and does not depend on having found the route at all.
- **`branchIndex` is meaningless without its parent order.** `intersectionCandidates(A, B)` and `(B, A)` are different canonical orders, so the same crossing has two different indices, and anything comparing branches across two points must map between them (the tool now does, by chordal match). This is a sharp edge for the codec, the tool, and anything that ever tries to compare or merge intersection points.

## Session 123 (V2 Session 25) — 2026-08-17

**Done**
- **Phase 120c** on `phase-120c-conic-defects`, three commits — three defects reported from `~/Documents/geometry/regula/debug`, each reproduced headlessly before anything was edited, and each fixed at its root. Suite **2371 green**, 32 goldens byte-identical, analyze clean, perf gate PASS.
- **Conics drew as polygons** (`square-ellipse.rgl`, `square-hyperbola.rgl`). `ConicShape.polylines` bounds its adaptive walk two ways, and **only `maxSamples` is a cost cap** — spending `maxDepth` emits the chord regardless of how far the curve strays from it, so a depth cap tight enough to bind is a silent *accuracy* failure while a sample cap that binds merely coarsens. Depth was the binding one. The sweep parameter is a pencil angle and its map to arc length spreads `|dX/dφ|` by ~1e7 on ordinary figures — about 24 bisections — against a cap of 12. Measured at the documents' own viewports: **10.4 px of sagitta against a 0.5 px tolerance** on the ellipse (105 px chords), **171.8 px** on the hyperbola's second branch (381 px chords). Neither came near `maxSamples`, at 4000 *or* at 200000. Raised to 32; cost 15 µs → 49 µs per conic per paint.
- **Intersection points accumulated into double points** (`ellipse-intersection-issue.rgl` — six points on one conic pair, four stored at `branchIndex: 1`). *(Session 124: this fix was necessary but not sufficient — a third `0..1` bound on the commit path kept the defect alive in the app.)* Branch adoption in `_traceAlong` wrote `matchedIndex` back only for `matchedIndex <= 1`. That cap was right while two candidates was all any carrier pair could produce and was left behind when Phase 120 made four reachable — Phase 120b had already raised `maxBranchCount` for the same reason. **It does not merely skip adoption past the cap; it makes adoption asymmetric**: a root landing at 0 or 1 writes back while one landing at 2 or 3 silently does not, so two branches converge onto one stored index and the next static recompute puts both points on the same root. Replaying the document, three of four points collapsed within two drag frames; with the cap tracking `maxBranchCount` the same drag keeps four distinct addresses throughout.
- Second half, in `IntersectionTool`: dedup classified existing points by **proximity**, which can only speak for a point that has a position. While the crossings are complex every tap looked unoccupied and stacked another object on a branch already built. Added a structural test — an `IntersectionPoint` on the same ordered pair at the same index *is* that intersection by construction — which dedups defined or not.
- **A there-and-back drag randomly returned the crossing on the far side** (`cinderella-jumping.rgl`). *(Session 124: the seam diagnosis below is right and stands; the alternation fix built on it was replaced by a constant half-plane, and the intended behaviour turned out to be the opposite — a round trip should trade the crossings.)* Oddness under path reversal is unchanged and still required — the return leg's `Im s > 0` is the outward leg's `Im t < 0`, so the legs cancel exactly when they detour on opposite sides in their own parameters. But **any ±1 rule on directions has a seam**, and `detourOrientation`'s sat on the horizontal axis: it read the sign of `dy` and consulted `dx` only when `dy` was *exactly* zero. "Drag two equal circles onto each other" is that gesture, so `dy` was pointer noise and each frame's half-plane came from the sign of the jitter. Measured on the rig: **exactly horizontal 60/60 correct; 0.05 px of y-jitter swapped about a third of runs**.
- The fix stops reading the direction. Each `IntersectionPoint` records the half-plane it last detoured in; the next detour takes the negation. Seam-free, and it generalizes by a parity argument — between two consecutive crossings of one singularity the path must leave and return, crossing every *other* singularity an even number of times, so a per-point counter alternates on that point's own crossings and the windings telescope to zero. Keyed **per point** (a global counter lets an unrelated drag advance someone else's parity) and deliberately **not gesture-scoped** (a there-and-back done as two drags is the common case; anything resetting at mouse-up would swap it). 300 randomized runs across three jitter levels and coarse frames: zero wrong-side outcomes.
- Decisions pinned in PLAN §"The detour half-plane is carried by memory, not read off the drag".

**Next**
- Merge `phase-120c-conic-defects`; then Phase 121 — old kernel deletion + convention unification. Two of its items moved this session: the deferred detour-orientation entry now distinguishes *mechanism* (replaced here) from *convention* (reversal-identity vs Cinderella's honest monodromy — still open, and now a choice between two working behaviours rather than one working and one flaky), and a new item asks whether a bail should clear the culprits' recorded half-plane.

**Gotchas**
- **`maxDepth` and `maxSamples` are not interchangeable knobs.** Lowering depth to "make conics cheaper" reintroduces exactly this bug, silently, and no golden will catch it — the golden scenes' conics sit near the origin at modest scale, where 12 was already enough. That is why Phase 119 shipped with it. Tune `maxSamples`.
- **Adoption's index cap must track `maxBranchCount`.** Any future widening of `branchIndex`'s range has to move both, and the failure mode of forgetting is not "adoption stops" — it is two points silently sharing a branch.
- **Alternation carries state and so is not self-correcting.** A memoryless rule recovers next frame; if a crossing detours going out and the return leg bails instead of detouring, the parity stays wrong from then on. Detours fired symmetrically on all 60 randomized subdivisions of the rig, so this is the rare path — but it is the first thing to suspect if branch swaps ever come back. Listed as a Phase 121 item.
- `detourOrientation` / `detourOrientation1D` are still live: the opening move for a collision with no history, and the locus sweeps' rule (a locus walks a deterministic scan, not a gesture). Both carry a note not to restore them as the standing rule.
- The reproductions are worth keeping in mind as a method: all three were pinned by replaying the *saved document's own numbers* — including its viewport, which is what made the sagitta figures meaningful — rather than by a synthetic rig. The jitter sweep in particular only reproduces because the frame subdivision is randomized; a fixed subdivision returns correctly every time and would have read as "not reproducible".
