## 2026-08-22 — session 163: JGEX's Prove menu, compared

Docs only, no code. A user put JGEX's *Prove* menu beside ours and asked three questions: what can we answer, why is concurrency missing, and why did selecting four segments offer nothing.

**What we can answer, and the two lists it separates into.** `predicate.dart` holds ten relations and `questions.dart` offers six from four selection shapes, against JGEX's seventeen items. The gaps have only three causes between them, all already-scheduled work: no *name* for the point a statement is about (tangency's touch point, concurrency's meeting point → Phase 153); no coefficient ring or length system (Special Angle, Angles Equation, Equal Product, Segment Equation → 152e's deferred boxes); and a selection being a `Set<String>`, which forecloses the *correspondence* `simtri`/`contri` need. Written up in PLAN §"JGEX's question list, compared".

**Concurrency is missing from JGEX's list too, and that is the answer.** In a point-tuple vocabulary three lines concurring is `coll(P, X, Y)` at the meeting point, and dually collinearity of three points. Sugar over an existing `IntersectionPoint` is thirty lines (Phase 159, numbered 156 then); a real `concurrent` predicate would buy refutation with *no* named point via a 3×3 determinant, and is held back as a vocabulary addition needing its own symmetry group.

**Two genuine findings behind the four-segment report.** First, `questions.dart:186` handles at most two carriers, so four segments falls straight through to empty — and the panel then renders *nothing at all*, no header and no reason, which is why a limitation read as breakage. Second, `eqangle`'s exclusion is justified as "eight points, or a correspondence a selection cannot express", and that reasoning is true of point selections and **false of carriers**: four segments are exactly `∠(s1,s2) = ∠(s3,s4)`, with the same three-pairing ambiguity already accepted for four points. The justification was over-applied.

**Tangent is an oversight, not a decision.** `TangentLine` emits nothing and is not in `hypotheses()`'s documented list of kinds that contribute nothing deliberately. Tangency needs no new predicate — `perp(O, T, T, P)` at a named touch point, with the radii `cong` already emitted — and the blocker is only that the touch point is computed inside `recompute` rather than being a `GeoPoint`. When the user has drawn the intersection, the emission is available today.

**The sequencing consequence, which is the part worth remembering.** Phase 152e deferred the length system on measured evidence (0 new `cong` on five fixtures, one `eqratio` in the whole corpus) — but that is a fact about the corpus. Tangent-length equality manufactures `cong` and power-of-a-point manufactures ratio chains, so **Phase 155 re-opens that measurement** and must come before it.

Four phases opened: **154** the mobile proof sheet (`main.dart:1451` pins it at half height with no `isScrollControlled` — a `DraggableScrollableSheet` with 0.5 as the *initial* size, plus threading a `ScrollController?` into the panel's three bare `ListView`s), **155** the tangent hypothesis, **156** the questions a selection cannot ask, **157** the correspondence questions. 154 and 155 have no dependencies; 156 wants 155 for its tangency chip; 157 is gated on deciding ordered-vs-panel-local correspondence before any code.

**Next:** 154 or 155 — 154 is the user-visible annoyance and is self-contained; 155 is the one that unblocks a deferred milestone box.

### Addendum — the correspondence answer was wrong, and JGEX's dialog corrects it

The entry above says a `Set<String>` selection cannot express a correspondence and names two ways out: order the selection (a change reaching every consumer) or add a pairing gesture. The user then showed JGEX's *Equal Angle* dialog — a relation dropdown, eight point slots grouped *First Set* / *Second Set*, a live count, *OK* disabled until full — and it is **neither**. JGEX does not order a selection; it does not read the selection at all. **The slot carries the order.**

So `selectionProvider` never changes, and Phase 160 (numbered 157 when this was written) is rewritten from "the correspondence questions, blocked on ordered selection" to **the question builder**: a `QuestionTemplate` of typed, grouped slots that produces the existing `ProverQuestion` unchanged — a producer in front of the prover, not a change to it. Three deliberate departures from JGEX's dialog, each with its reason in PLAN §"The question builder": a line slot accepts a **carrier** and expands to all witness pairs (JGEX makes the user name two points on a line the app already knows); **tap-to-fill** rather than dropdowns, since eight dropdowns is bad at thirty points and unusable on a phone — and clicks are where the ordering physically comes from; and **refute before OK**, because `DiagramFilter` answers a full template with no prover run, so a false statement should be caught in the builder rather than after a run.

The builder subsumes `simtri`/`contri`/Equilateral/Bisect and makes the tangency ask and a future `concurrent` predicate reachable as soon as their engine side lands. The selection chips stay and stay first: "what can I ask about *this*" and "I want to ask *this* — about what" are different questions, and the chips are one gesture where the builder is a dozen. Phase 159's four-carrier `eqangle` (numbered 156 then) is now explicitly the fast path rather than the whole answer.

## 2026-08-22 — session 164: half the screen is where the proof sheet opens, not where it stops

Phase 154, on branch `phase-154-proof-sheet-phone`. The user's report from session 163: on a phone the proof panel rises to half the screen and stops there.

**The stated reason was right about the default and silent about the ceiling.** `_openProofSheet` built a bare `FractionallySizedBox(heightFactor: 0.5)` with the comment "read alongside the figure, not instead of it" — a good reason for where the sheet *opens* and no reason at all for where it *stops*. So 0.5 becomes `DraggableScrollableSheet`'s `initialChildSize`, between a 0.25 minimum and a 0.95 maximum, and the first impression is unchanged.

**Two things had to be true together, and either alone does nothing.** `isScrollControlled: true` on the route — without it `showModalBottomSheet` constrains itself to half the screen and the maximum is unreachable however the child is built — and the controller reaching a scrollable, since a `DraggableScrollableSheet` resizes from the scroll gestures of the scrollable it hands its controller to. `ProofPanel` takes an optional `ScrollController?`; the docked call site passes none and is unchanged.

**The fourth body the phase's plumbing box did not count.** It named three `ListView`s. The fourth is `_note` — a bare `Padding`, and the body the panel *opens* on ('Nothing proved yet'), so leaving it unscrollable would have meant the first thing a reader sees is the one thing they cannot drag taller. It is now a `SingleChildScrollView`, which is not about its length. One body shows at a time, so the single controller is never attached twice.

**`shouldCloseOnMinExtent: false` is the decision worth remembering.** It makes the minimum a *tuck* rather than a dismissal, and that divides the two gestures cleanly: the content resizes the sheet, the drag handle and the scrim dismiss it. A reader glancing at the figure does not lose the proof they were in the middle of. It also answers the phase's third box — the back arrow is in the header, outside the scrollable and pinned, so shrinking cannot hide it; *Keep going* is inside the list and is reached by scrolling.

**Six tests, none of which names a fraction.** The phase warned that a test asserting a `heightFactor` would pin the shape of the bug, so they assert the gesture: drag up and the panel grows past half the screen, drag down and it shrinks without being dismissed, the empty state grows too. Mutation-checked both ways — restoring the old `FractionallySizedBox` fails four of the six, and dropping the controller threading alone fails the same four, which is what says both halves are load-bearing. One test-writing trap caught in the act: `scrollUntilVisible` passes vacuously on these lists, because `ListView(children:)` builds every child and the finder matches a row sitting at y=821 on an 800-px screen. `ensureVisible` plus a rect assertion is what actually checks reachability.

Suite 3113 green (3107 + 6), analyze clean, browser gate 13, `flutter build web --release` compiles.

**Not done: the look.** The phase's last gate is "looked at on a narrow window", and this is a layout claim, so the widget test is evidence about layout and gestures but not about the renderer. The Chrome extension was not connected this session, so browser automation could not drive it. A release build is served at `http://127.0.0.1:8731/` for the user to narrow and drag.

**Next:** the look, then Phase 155 — the tangent hypothesis, which is the one that re-opens 152e's deferred length-system measurement.

### Addendum — the look was attempted, and the way it failed is worth keeping

The extension connected on the second try and the release build loaded, but `resize_window` silently no-ops on a **macOS-fullscreen** Chrome window: it reports success every time while the page's own numbers say otherwise — `innerWidth` pinned at the screen width, `screenX/screenY` at 0, and `outerWidth`/`outerHeight` coming back *smaller* than the inner size, which cannot happen on a real window. That last one is the tell.

The workaround tried — pinning `flutter-view` to 430 px — is a **dead end, and instructively so**: it clips the view without changing what Flutter reports. The app bar stayed at its 56-px Material height instead of the 48-px `_phoneBarHeight`, which says `compactPanels` was still false; the panel would have docked rather than opened as a sheet, so it was the wide layout seen through a narrow hole. Any future attempt to fake a phone viewport in the browser should check the bar height first — it is the cheapest signal that the gate actually engaged.

The look is deferred rather than done, at the user's call. Take Chrome out of fullscreen, then `resize_window` works.

## 2026-08-22 — session 165: Phase 155, the tangent hypothesis

Merged to `main` with Phase 154 (`7791c2f`, `e72fc8d`) and pushed. Suite 3128, analyze clean, browser gate 13; all three workflows green (CI 4m30s, Benchmarks 36s, Deploy 2m35s), so both phases are live.

**The gap was an oversight, and the blocker was not real.** `TangentLine` emitted nothing and was not on `hypotheses()`'s documented list of kinds that emit nothing deliberately — which is how the gap should be read. The stated blocker was that the touch point is computed inside `recompute` and is never a `GeoPoint`. It does not have to be: **a point the construction puts on both the tangent and its circle *is* the touch point**, because a tangent meets its circle exactly once. So `perp(O, T, T, P)` for every other named `P` on the line. Two things it needed beyond that: a **named centre** (a `ThreePointCircle` has none, so a drawn `CircleCenter` is the fallback — without it the most natural tangency figure emits nothing), and a **circle-by-construction guard**, since the tangent to a general conic at `T` is not perpendicular to `T→centre`.

**The audit found two more, and one of them arrives by a different road.** `PolarLine` and `RadicalAxisLine` had never been assessed. Both contribute, so neither joins the deliberate-silence list. The polar twice: perpendicular to the centre→pole join, and **La Hire** — a named point on both the polar and the circle is a point of contact, so the tangent there passes through the pole, which reaches the tangency fact **with no `TangentLine` in the figure at all**. That one has a trap worth remembering because getting it wrong emits a *falsehood* rather than nothing: the pair's other point must be the **pole**, not another point of the polar. Checked, not asserted — the filter accepts `perp(O, T0, T0, pole)` and rejects `perp(O, T0, T0, T1)`.

**The fixture came before the rules, deliberately.** 152e's four zero measurements were every one of them limited by the corpus, so measuring new rules against that same corpus would have repeated the mistake. `test/fixtures/tangent-chase.rgl` (v1, 11 objects): two tangents from an external `P` with both contacts drawn, plus a secant. Its secant's far point uses branch 0 and means it — `A` is a defining point of the secant *and* on the circle, so Phase 135's deflation divides it out.

**One rule kept, one measured and dropped.** `tangent_lengths` — `perp(o,s,s,p) & perp(o,t,t,p) & cong(o,s,o,t) => cong(p,s,p,t)` — reads as **Pythagoras**, not as a circle theorem, which is why its premises name no circle. 0 new facts on all five older fixtures; exactly three on the new one, including the chord of contact `perp(s,t,o,p)` that `perp_bisector` reaches from it. `tangent_chord` is a genuine theorem that fires, and is **dropped**: same two substantive facts by the longer road, plus 13 eqangles at ~2× the run time, and on `apatitos-topos` **3 facts every one of them an eqangle**. Sixteen eqangles corpus-wide with no consumer — 152e restated, `para_coll`'s precedent.

**Two findings that outlast the phase.**

*Power of the point was not what tangency was missing.* `simtri(p,s,a,p,b,s)` is **true** in the figure — but the two triangles are oppositely oriented, so the shared angle at `p` that `aa_simtri`'s second premise wants is **false as a mod-π eqangle**. That is the direct/reflected split M-P1 defers, so no rule added to this table reaches around it. The fixture now pins the difference between *the table is short a theorem* and *the vocabulary cannot say it*.

*152e's length-system deferral stands, with a better reason than it had.* Tangency **does** change the corpus — `tangent_lengths` manufactures a `cong` that was not there — and the closure still buys nothing: **`entailed == cong` on all six fixtures**, so the stored facts already *are* the pairwise closure of their classes and a union-find has no work left. That is stronger than "the corpus does not exercise it". Kept as a tripwire (`length_closure_upside_test.dart`) rather than written up and discarded, because the claim it guards is a deferral: the day `newCong` goes non-zero, that failure is the signal to build the thing.

**Gotchas worth carrying.** `provoleas2.json` never reaches a fixpoint, so any corpus-wide measurement must run it at a budget rather than at quiescence — an uncapped `run()` is a hang, not a measurement. And `scrollUntilVisible` passes *vacuously* on `ListView(children:)`, which builds every child: the finder matches an off-screen row and nothing scrolls. `ensureVisible` plus a rect assertion is what checks reachability.

**Next:** Phase 159 — the questions a selection cannot ask (numbered 156 when this was written) — the tangency chip is now unblocked, and its cheapest box (the empty state saying *why*) has no dependencies at all. Phase 153's third box needs a new unreachable statement: `tangent-chase.rgl`'s theorem was a candidate for about an hour, and 155d made it reachable.

## 2026-08-24 — session 167: Phase 156, and the measurement that renamed the freeze

**Done — Phase 156 end to end on `phase-156-stop-resume`, four commits.** Suite **3140** green (3128 + 12, one re-shaped), analyze clean, browser gate 13. Session 166's uncommitted doc edits (Phases 156–158 opened, old 156/157 renumbered 159/160) were committed first on `main` — they were sitting in the working tree with no STATUS entry.

- **Stop and resume, both from hooks already in the code.** A cancel flag read where `stopWhen` already is, once per pass; a stop *publishes* the prefix in the identical shape a spent budget publishes (`ProverReady(reachedFixpoint: false)`, or an undecided answer), so *Keep going* resumes it with no new arm on `ProverState`. Cancellation is deliberately not supersession — `_generation` drops results, a stop must deliver one. And `prove()` gained `ask()`'s reuse guard, generalized to `_heldRevision` over `ProverReady` *and* `ProverAnswered` (excluding `ProverRunning`: reusing an engine an in-flight run may be driving would interleave two `runChunked` loops over it). Mutation-checked both ways: a no-op `stop()` fails exactly the three stop tests, dropping the guard fails exactly the two resume tests.
- **The chunk measured, and the box's premise was wrong — which is the phase's real finding.** `benchmark/prover_chunk_bench.dart` (kept): the worst pass is **flat across every candidate budget** on `apatitos-topos` (1.4 s) and `tangent-chase` (2.5 s), because their cost is the join enumeration that yields no candidate — advanced without being charged, so no application budget bounds it. Where applications are the cost the budget binds: `provoleas2` 53 → 24 ms at chunk 250, the knee. `proverChunkBudget` 1000 → 250 with the table in its doc comment; the unbounded pass is **Phase 161**, opened. The prologue: 47–430 µs everywhere — a floor that needs no lowering.
- **Progress while running**: `Prover.runChunked` gained `onPass` (once per pass, final included, never on an entry-check return); `ProverRunning` carries applications, republished per pass behind the generation guard; the body says 'Deriving… N steps so far.' A resumed run's first publish starts from the held count.
- **`clear()` got its caller** — File ▸ New / Open both drop the held engine with the document.

**Next.** Phase 157 (a fact in words), then 158 (the grouped derived list) — both open with scope written, and 157 is a named dependency of Phase 159's chips. Phase 161 is open and unscheduled. Carried: Phase 153 (auxiliary construction); the id-based fact transfer for `Isolate.run`; M-P4's point-merge *Show why*; Phase 139's open box; Android/iOS smokes; the pure deflation route; the direct/reflected `simtri` split.

**Gotchas.**

- **`tangent-chase.rgl` runs 5.7 s to quiescence** in the provider configuration — the newest fixture is now the slowest document by far, and nothing had timed it before. It is the uncharged-join mechanism, not the chunk size; recorded in Phase 161.
- **The chunk bench times `run(maxApplications: chunk)` as a pass proxy** — one call is one pass because the per-call budget is spent whole in the first pass. If `run`'s loop shape changes, the bench's equivalence breaks silently.
- **A widget test that wants a run held mid-flight needs a document that outlives one chunk.** Varignon completes inside its first pass, so its running state is never on screen; the Stop test uses `perp-true-unproved.rgl` and holds the run on its yield timer (`pump` without settling), which is Phase 152d's timer lesson used in the other direction.
- **`find.text` misses list rows below the fold** in the docked panel's `ListView` — the foot must be dragged into view first. Session 165's `scrollUntilVisible`-is-vacuous note is about the *sheet*; the docked list lazily materializes.
- The existing 'publishes a running state before it yields' pin assumed exactly one `ProverRunning` per run; per-pass progress makes that N. Re-shaped to first-state-is-running rather than deleted.

### Addendum — the browser found what the suite could not, twice in one day

The user's live report ("scrolling in the browser gets stuck at various points") arrived with a `flutter run` stack that named the culprit outright: `Tooltip ← … ← _ListTile`, this session's own raw-spelling tooltips. The mechanism: `Tooltip` shows on hover **immediately** by default, so a stationary pointer over a scrolling lazy list attaches and detaches an overlay for every row that passes under it, and stable 3.41.9's `RawTooltip`/`OverlayPortal` (the descendant of flutter/flutter#133545, fixed in 3.13 and regressed since) lets the mouse tracker hit-test the overlay's never-laid-out deferred box — one exception per pointer event, which *is* the stuck scrolling.

Fixed in a third commit on the branch: `rawSpellingTooltip` (500 ms `waitDuration`, chips wrapped in it too since `ActionChip.tooltip` offers no wait) plus `Tooltip.dismissAllToolTips()` on any scroll notification over the panel body. One pinning test, both guards mutation-checked. Suite **3167**.

Three things worth keeping beyond the fix. **The crash is unreachable from every harness we have**: the VM probe (real hover gesture, wheel scroll under it) passed against the *broken* code, and the same probe on `--platform chrome` deadlocked awaiting `prove()` before reaching the gesture — the browser-gate memory ("a green suite is not evidence about the renderer") now has a pointer-events example, not just a rasterizer one. **A mid-mutation `git checkout` of an uncommitted fix silently reverts the fix itself** — mutation check 2 first "passed" vacuously because the whole feature was gone; commit the fix *before* mutation-checking, and make the mutation script assert its replace actually changed the file. **Piping a long-running server through `head` kills it** at line 30 with exit 0 — the flutter run web-server died looking healthy.

The browser look is the one open box: extension not connected this session (second occurrence — session 164's addendum was the first). Debug build at `http://127.0.0.1:8731/` — hover a derived row, wait for the raw tooltip, wheel-scroll; the console must stay free of `Cannot hit test`.

### Addendum — Phase 159b, `eqangle` from four carriers

**Done on the same branch, one code commit (`28cc2fe`).** Suite **3189** green (3186 + 3), analyze clean, browser gate 13. Still not merged.

- **The box's analogy is right in count and wrong in shape, and the test is what said so.** "Three pairings, as four points already offer for `para`" reads as sides `(01|23)`, `(02|13)`, `(03|12)`. But `eqangle`'s transpose symmetry (M-P2a) makes `∠(c0,c1) = ∠(c2,c3)` *the same fact* as `∠(c0,c2) = ∠(c1,c3)` — so that set offers one statement twice and misses one. Read as linear equations over line angles, `θ1 − θ0 = θ3 − θ2` is `θ1 + θ2 = θ0 + θ3`: a statement is a **partition of the four lines into two pairs with equal angle sums**, three of them, and the orientation of the second side is what distinguishes them. `askableQuestions` offers `[0,1,2,3]`, `[0,1,3,2]`, `[0,2,3,1]`, and the test canonicalizes all six orientations anchored on `c0` and pins that they land on exactly the three offered facts — plus the naive duplicate pinned as equal, so the comment stays a fact about the group and not about the rig. Mutating to the naive set fails that test alone.
- **Spellings as the two-carrier shape does them**: the product of every group's witness pairs, refusing a line against itself on either side (`_degenerate`, existing) and the same two lines on both sides (`_sameLines`, new — the four-carrier form of "two carriers naming the same two points relate nothing"; dropping it fails exactly the double-twin test). A *single* twinned carrier does **not** collapse anything: `ax` beside `ab` is a different spelling of one line, and without the carrier index nothing here can know it — the documented "no index, nothing is trivial" limitation, now visible from the chips as well as the derived list.
- **The end-to-end verdict is DD's, not AR's.** On a cyclic rig (P, Q, A, B on one circle, segments PA PB QA QB) the reading `∠APB = ∠AQB` comes back **proved** via `inscribed_angle`, and the other two readings **refuted** with no run. On Varignon, though, the natural four-side question (`∠(MN,NP) = ∠(QP,MQ)`, two parallels) is *unproved*: the DD core has no `para & para ⇒ eqangle` rule, and the run's only `eqangle`s there are the four trivial `isosceles_base` ones. That is exactly the gap the next box (the AR ask) is for, measured from the asking side before deciding.
- The hint now reads "two or four lines"; `askableQuestions`'s doc comment says where Phase 148's exclusion still holds (point selections) and where it did not.

**Next.** Phase 159's third box — answer the `eqangle` ask against AR's entailment, a membership query; Varignon's four sides are the rig where DD says *unproved* and AR should not. Then concurrency as sugar, the tangency ask.

Because `derivation_check.dart` and `AngleChase.of` both go through `equationOf`, an `eqangle` recorded as an `angle_arithmetic` step verifies and renders as a chase with no change to either.
- **`Prover.resolve(fact)`**: the database, or on a miss the closure — and the one entailed statement is *recorded* as an `angle_arithmetic` step (filter-screened, premises present, `database.add`), so `Proof.of` has a derivation and `verify` a certificate. The enumeration ban in `_publish` stands: an ask is a single membership query. Mutation (skip the closure branch) fails exactly the three new tests.

The other four fixtures have fewer than four carriers. Path kept.

- **Concurrency**: every pair of the three carriers with a *named* point structurally on both, against every witness pair of the third that the meeting point is not part of — all spellings of one statement. The degenerate shape is real: three lines through P with no other name on any of them phrase nothing, because every pair on every third line contains P. Refusing that is what keeps "P, P, C are collinear" off the chip row (mutation-checked).

The circle selection is admitted only in this one shape — a circle with anything else still phrases nothing.
- **End to end in the panel**: the pencil's concurrency and the tangent both come back *proved* (each is a hypothesis of its figure — the plumbing is what the box owed, not a theorem), the secant *refuted* with no run.

**The phase in one paragraph.** Five working boxes, five commits, 3185 → 3208. The hint (159a), the four-carrier `eqangle` with the corrected pairing count (159b), the closure-backed ask that turned out to be the first positive AR measurement rather than the fifth negative (159c), and the two sugars (159d/e). What the panel *offers* went from six of the vocabulary's ten to seven (`eqangle` joined) plus two sentences the vocabulary has no word for. Still absent by design: `eqratio`, `simtri`, `contri`, and the compound questions — all Phase 160's, the builder.

**Gotchas.**

- A carrier's name in a reading is its *first known pair* — the defining pair for a segment or ray, else the first two named points on it in construction order. - `LineThroughTwoPoints` pairs contain the defining points *as non-length* pairs; the concurrency reading still names them, which is what you want.

## 2026-08-24 — session 170 (continued): Phase 162, a question reads as spelled

**Done — Phase 162 end to end on `phase-159-unaskable-questions` (same branch, after Phase 159's merge; two code commits, `ebd0a4f` + the pin).** Suite **3215** green (3208 + 7), analyze clean, browser gate 13, wasm release build compiles.

**The report.** On `tangent-chord.rgl` (now `test/fixtures/tangent-chord.rgl`, v1) the four segments of "∠ECB = ∠CDB" offered "angles ECD and CBD are equal" — false as magnitudes — and two chips beginning "the angle from EC to DB…" faded off the chip's edge. All three were the *right statements*: `questionLabel` read `readFact(Fact.of(canonical))`, and `eqangle`'s transpose symmetry canonicalized `∠(BC,CE) = ∠(BD,DC)` (the theorem, 47.4° = 47.4°) into `∠(EC,DC) = ∠(BC,DB)` — the same directed fact mod π, whose three-point prose names angles of 73° and 107°. **A fact reads canonical because it is its orbit; a question is a spelling the user chose, and reads as spelled.** That is the phase's one decision.

- **`readPredicate`** beside `readFact`, one `_read(kind, points)` under both; `questionLabel` and the asked proof's goal row use it (`_proofRows(goalLabel:)`), the tooltip still shows the canonical fact. Pinned both in `fact_naming_test` (the transpose is one `Fact` and two sentences) and through the panel on the fixture (mutating the chip back to `readFact(Fact.of(…))` fails exactly that test).
- **The four-carrier orientation is chosen** (`_bestOrientation`): of a statement's eight spellings, prefer three-point on both sides, then the one whose *magnitude* reading is true in the figure (`point.position`, as the filter reads it), first-listed on ties. Without the magnitude preference construction order decides: on the fixture's own order the true reading happens to come first (the mutant *passed* the fixture tests — caught only because the check was run), so a second rig adds the carriers as BC, BD, CE, DC, where the transpose "CBD and ECD" comes first and only the magnitude check picks a true spelling ("CDB and ECB" there — which of the statement's four magnitude-true spellings wins is enumeration order, and the test accepts any); mutating the bonus away fails exactly that test.
- **Chips wrap — and Material's `Chip` cannot.** Three attempts, each measured: `Text(maxLines: null)` inherits the chip's `DefaultTextStyle(maxLines: 1)` and does nothing; a fresh `DefaultTextStyle`, and a plain `Text(maxLines: 8)`, both make *every* chip 276 px wide and two lines tall, because `_RenderChip` sizes itself from an unconstrained raw layout and then lays the label out again inside that width minus its own padding. So `QuestionChip`: `Material` + `InkWell` + padded `Text`, the action chip's look, sizing to its text. Widget-test finders moved from `ActionChip` to `QuestionChip`.
- **The chip block is capped** at `ProofPanel.questionsShare` (40 %) of the panel and scrolls within the cap; the tooltip-dismissing `NotificationListener` now wraps the whole panel since the chips scroll too. This is a real phone concern, not only a test one: four selected points offer ten chips, and ten wrapped chips at 320 px are the whole sheet.
- **Not fixed, and the reason the user's goal still comes back *unproved*:** the run reaches 7 facts and no route to the tangent–chord angle. Phase 155 wrote, measured and **dropped** `tangent_chord` — reachable another way on *that* fixture, not on this one, where the inscribed-angle road needs a fourth point on the circle. Reinstating it in a cheaper premise shape than `cong × cong` is the next phase to open if the user wants this theorem proved; the fixture is in place for it.

**The test-font finding, worth its own line.** In widget tests every glyph of the default font is 14 px wide, so "MN is parallel to PQ" is 280 px — wider than the 276 px docked `Wrap` — and wraps where the real app never would. That is what turned ten Varignon chips into 634 px and left the answer's lazy list 11 px tall with its verdict unbuilt: the failures read as "the ask broke" and were the chip block eating the body. Every chip tap in the panel tests now `ensureVisible`s first (the block scrolls), and chip strings are computed through `askableQuestions` + `questionLabel` rather than from the canonical fact's point order — session 168's gotcha inverted: **a chip's point order is now the question's, not the canonical fact's.**

**Next.** Merge (this session). Then either the `tangent_chord` rule (the user's actual theorem) or Phase 160/161 as before. One follow-up recorded and not done: derived-list rows and proof steps still read canonical `eqangle` facts, so a *listed* angle fact can read as a magnitude-false three-point sentence for the same reason; `readFact` could prefer a three-point spelling of the orbit without numerics, and a magnitude-aware one with them — not started.

**Gotchas.**

- A carrier's name in a reading is its first known pair in construction order (the tangent here reads `CE`: C is its pole and was added first). Fine for chips; the builder's line slot (Phase 160) will want a real carrier name.
- `_sameMagnitude` compares to 1e-6 rad on unperturbed positions only — it is a *preference*, not a claim; the filter is still what decides truth.

## 2026-08-24 — session 171: Phase 163, the tangent–chord rule and the rule it cost

- **The downstream cost was `eqangle_transitive`, alone.** No shared variable between its premises, so 128 × 128 orbit forms per pair of facts, uncharged. The provider's cap comment said that document was "still deriving after 200 000"; corrected, cap kept (the next document is not the corpus).
- **`tangent_chord` in**: the user's figure proves in 5 ms by one step over the tangency and two radii, `verify()` empty, pinned to have no route without the rule; the panel chip comes back *proved*. Per-rule rig pins the swapped mod-π reading as refuted. `tangent-chase` 388 ms quiescent with it.

- **Phase 161 repriced**: worst pass at chunk 250 is 72 ms on `apatitos-topos` (was 1.4 s) and 297 ms on `tangent-chase` (was 2.5 s). The remaining over-frame pass is the `eqangle × eqangle` joins of `aa_simtri`/`sas_simtri`.

**Gotchas.**

- A measurement rig that calls `Prover.resolve` *records* what it resolves — my first fact counts were 15 high on `provoleas2` for exactly that reason. Count before asking.
- Rules with a repeated variable (`aa_simtri`'s `eqangle(a,b,a,c,…)`) are cheap despite two 128-form premises, because most pivot forms fail at the pivot binding and never reach the join. It is the *all-distinct* pattern that is quadratic in forms.
- `length_closure_upside_test` had run `provoleas2` at a 20 000 budget "because it never converges"; it converges at 25 826 now, so the test runs everything to quiescence and asserts it.

## 2026-08-24 — session 172: Phase 164, one line under two objects

**Started as a bug report, not a phase.** The tangent–chord theorem proved on the VM and in `flutter run`, and the user reported it *not* proving on the deployed site. Everything headless said the deploy was fine: the deployed `main.dart.wasm` and `.js` both contain `tangent_chord` and not `eqangle_transitive`; the service worker is Flutter's self-unregistering stub; the provider's own path (probe → seed → `runChunked` → resolve) compiled with the deploy's exact command (`flutter build web --wasm --release`) proves it in headless Chrome under both the wasm and the dart2js arm. Then the extension connected, the fixture went into the deployed app through the *Open* dialog (memory: `load-fixture-into-deployed-app`), and the user's symptom reproduced on the first try — **"Nothing to ask about this selection"** — with the tab verifiably running the new build.

**The cause was in the document, and it was the tangent tool's.** `tangent-chord.rgl` holds two `TangentLine`s at C, `c` and `d`, because the tool emits both branches unconditionally and from a point on the circle both are the tangent there. E is glued to `d`. A canvas click on the line lands on `c` or `d` by a last-bit difference in their coefficients (the hit-tester breaks only *exact* ties by insertion order), and `c` carries only C — no witness pair, no angle question. Hiding `c` and repeating the clicks gave the three chips and the proof. "Works locally" was a different click, or a different last bit.

**Done — Phase 164 on `phase-164-coincident-tangent`, one code commit (`a73d252`).** Suite **3229** green (3219 + 10), analyze clean, browser gate 13. Merged to `main` this session.

- `coincidentCarriers(a, b)` and `pointsOnCarrier(objects, carrier)` in `incidence.dart`, structural and symmetric, zero epsilon; `askableQuestions` and `hypotheses()` read through the latter. No fact count on any fixture moved.
- The tangent tool emits one line from a point on the circle.
- PLAN gained §"One line under two objects is one line".

**Next.** Phase 164's deployed look is done (same clicks, three chips, proved). Then Phase 160 (the question builder) or the two recorded measurements (delete `para_transitive`; re-measure the ratio closure on `provoleas2`). Carried: `readFact` on listed `eqangle`s can still read magnitude-false — visible in this session's screenshot as step `[4]` reading "angles ECD and CBD" under a question that read "BCE and BDC".

**Gotchas.**

- "Works locally, not on the site" is not evidence about the build until the *same document* has been loaded on the site. The file-input route (patch `HTMLInputElement.prototype.click`, catch the input, append it, `file_upload`) makes that a two-minute check; `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` gives `main.dart.wasm`'s decoded size, which identifies the build.
- Headless Chrome without a GPU makes the bootstrap fall back to the dart2js arm silently (the console says "Falling back to CPU-only rendering", the source column says `main.dart.js`). `--use-angle=swiftshader --enable-unsafe-swiftshader` gets the wasm arm; Chrome then does not exit on its own.
- `flutter test --platform chrome --wasm` hung for eight minutes with no output on a test that ran in 3 s under dart2js; killed, not diagnosed. A release build of a scratch entrypoint under `lib/` served to headless Chrome was the faster road — and the entrypoint has to be deleted before anything ships.

## 2026-08-25 — session 173: Phase 160, the question builder

**Done — Phase 160 end to end on `phase-160-question-builder`, three code commits.** Suite **3274** green (3229 + 45), analyze clean, browser gate 13, wasm release build compiles. Merged to `main` this session.

- **160a — the spelling rules in one place.** `questions.dart`'s `_Pair`/`_Group`/`groupFor` and the relation, concurrency, tangency and eqangle builders moved to `question_spellings.dart` (`WitnessPair`, `CarrierGroup`, four `…Question` functions) so the chips and the builder spell one statement one way. **The move found a defect**: `cong` was spelled through every witness pair, so `|AB| = |CD|` on a segment carrying a third point `X` also asked `cong(A,X,C,D)` — a different statement; a proof of it would have answered *proved*. `cong` reads its bounding pair only now (pinned); `perp`/`para` still read every pair.
- **160b — `QuestionTemplate` and `QuestionDraft`, pure.** The template is an enum (exhaustive over `PredicateKind` by test, plus `concurrent`/`tangent` sugars). A *line* slot is one carrier **or two points** (so `eqangle` is four line slots: JGEX's eight points, or every witness pair); a *segment* slot must bound a length. The draft is immutable — `tap` (a refused tap returns the same instance), `put`, `clear`, `seeded`, `question(objects)`. The correspondence test uses a scalene triangle and its medial triangle: the rows' reading holds under `DiagramFilter`, the swapped one is refuted with no run.
- **160c — the builder reaches the user.** `questionDraftProvider` (the open draft; deleted objects empty their slot) and `draftCheckProvider` (the verdict before *OK*, by the filter alone). `QuestionBuilderBar` **over the canvas on every layout** — on a phone the proof sheet is modal and covers the figure the builder is filled from, so the bar cannot live in the panel; the sheet closes when the builder opens from it and reopens on *Ask*. Select-mode canvas taps fill the next slot and leave the selection alone; every slot chip is also a menu of what fits it, with *Clear*. The panel header gained *Ask a question* (a template menu), and the unaskable hint points at it.
- **Compound questions decided against** (PLAN §"The builder as built"): the answer is three-way about the figure, and a split compound would need a fourth answer about the question's shape. Equilateral is two `cong` asks; Bisect is the `midp` or `eqangle` template.

**Next.** Phase 161 (the uncharged pass; one decision box) or the two recorded measurements (delete `para_transitive`; re-measure the ratio closure on `provoleas2`). Phase 153 (auxiliary construction) is the larger open item. Carried: `readFact` on listed `eqangle`s can still read magnitude-false; Phase 157's browser look still unverified; the builder's deployed look is unverified too (the widget tests cover both layouts, the browser did not).

**Gotchas.**

- `DiagramFilter.probe` perturbs *free* points independently, so six free points are never a theorem — a rig for "this holds under the filter" needs the second figure *derived* from the first (midpoints, here). My first simtri rig was two free triangles and refuted its own true statement.
- A `Segment` always names itself (its ends are its defining pair whatever `objects` holds), so "a carrier with no named points" needs a two-point *line* handed fewer objects than it was built from.
- With points selected, the panel's chips and the bar's status can carry the same sentence; scope widget finders to `QuestionBuilderBar.barKey`.
- `draftCheckProvider` re-probes on every construction notification while a complete draft is open — a drag pays a probe per frame then. Accepted and documented at the provider; the alternative (a stale verdict) is worse.
- A python edit script that asserts mid-way writes nothing: check for the file's *formatted* text before matching, or the first edits silently vanish with the failed one.

## 2026-08-25 — session 174: 152e's last box — the ratio half, measured, and the deferral ended

**Done — one code commit on `phase-152e-ratio-closure`.** `length_closure_upside_test.dart` gains the deferred system itself in test-local form: `_LengthSpan`, a ℚ row reduction over segment log-lengths (`cong` → `l₁ − l₂ = 0`, `eqratio` → `l₁ − l₂ − l₃ + l₄ = 0`, `midp` → the equal halves plus an `l_ab − l_ma = ln 2` row with `ln 2` as one more variable), and a second test that reads every `cong` and `eqratio` it entails beyond the database, screened through the filter, pinned per fixture.

- **The finding.** On `provoleas2.json` the closure entails **one new `cong`, `|AB| = |LO|`** — from the intercept fact `|MO|/|ON| = |OB|/|LO|` and the similar-triangle fact `|MA|/|AO| = |AO|/|AB|` beside the midpoint `cong`s, an integer combination with a coefficient 2 on `l_AO` that no union-find can form — and **43 `eqratio`s** chained through it; 2 `eqratio`s on `perp-true-unproved.rgl`; nothing on the other four; **0 refused by the filter**. So 152e's premise ("the ratio half has no input") is gone and the deferral ends: **Phase 165 — the length system** is opened in TODO with the shape the measurement fixes.
- **Two things the measurement decided about the build.** The midpoint constant is not what the chain needs — identical counts without the `ln 2` row — so DDAR's constant column can wait. And the payoff is a `cong`, which has DD consumers, so the length side *publishes* `cong` and answers `eqratio` on ask: the opposite split from the angle side, by the same rule (publish what has a consumer).
- **The rig's one trick, worth keeping**: an `eqratio` over four segments is entailed exactly when the residuals of `l₁ − l₂` and `l₃ − l₄` agree, so ordered pairs are bucketed by residual and the entailed statements read off each bucket — n² reductions, not n⁴ — which needs the residual to be a *canonical* coset representative, i.e. every pivot variable eliminated, not only the leading one. "Novel `eqratio`" excludes what pairs of stored `cong`s already say (`|AM|/|MB| = |MO|/|AO|` with all four equal), which was 57 of the 100 on `provoleas2`.
- Docs: 152e's three boxes ticked with the numbers, PLAN §"AR is a ℤ-module" length paragraph extended, Phase 165 written. Gates: analyze clean, suite **3275** green (3274 + 1), browser gate untouched (no `lib/` change).

**Next.** Phase 165 (the length system, four boxes, the angle side as template) is now the highest-value open engine item and has a regression waiting (`cong(A, B, L, O)` on `provoleas2`). Otherwise Phase 161's decision box, the `para_transitive` deletion, or Phase 153. Carried: `readFact` on listed `eqangle`s can still read magnitude-false; Phase 157's and the builder's browser looks unverified.

**Gotchas.**

- `provoleas2.json`'s point names are not the ones session 171's STATUS used: the midpoint of `AB` is **`O`**, the midpoint of `AO` is **`M`**, and `N` is the third vertex of the equilateral triangle `AON` — so Phase 163's "`|MA|/|AO| = |AO|/|AB|` beside `|AO|/|AN| = |ON|/|AO|`" was read off ids, not names. Name points through `describePoint`, not from memory.
- Three of the five stored `eqratio`s there are the similar-triangle spellings of `AON ~ ONA`, which together say the triangle is equilateral — content the `cong`s already hold, which is why the union-find was already saturated and the chain needed an *intercept* fact to buy anything.
- The python edit-script gotcha from session 173 bit again twice: match the *formatted* text, and a header rewrite that slices from a doc comment silently drops the imports above it. And a library doc comment must sit above `library;`, which must sit above every import.

Read the *first* failure in that file; the rest are cascade. (Whether the callback should be cancelled on dispose is a real question, left open — it only bites when a test dies mid-way.)

## 2026-08-25 — session 175: Phase 165, the length system

**Done — five code commits on `phase-165-length-closure`, phase complete (all five boxes).** The ℚ half of AR, built on the angle side's shape and sharing none of its arithmetic.

- **`length_closure.dart`** — homogeneous rows over segment log-lengths, ℚ Gaussian elimination, provenance from the first line. Three decisions: **no constant term** (equalities and ratios are scale-invariant, so a length system cannot be inconsistent — `AngleAddOutcome.contradiction` has no counterpart, and `ln 2` enters as a *variable* the day a document wants the 1:2); **the basis is kept fully reduced, not echelon**, which is what makes `residual` canonical and without which 152e's n² `eqratio` trick misses pairs (mutation-checked: disabling back-substitution fails exactly the two tests that pin it); **nothing shared with the angle side**, because a ℚ pivot reaching θ is a soundness bug no test here would see.
- **`length_translation.dart` + `Prover` + `derivation_check.dart`** — publishes `cong`, answers `eqratio` on ask. The opposite split from the angle side by the same rule (publish what has a consumer and what the enumeration can afford). Two asymmetries: `midp` may be **cited and not concluded** (equal distances do not put a point on a segment — `coll`'s treatment next door), and there is **no shared-point refusal**, since `|AB| = |AC|` is an isoceles triangle where `para` through one point is a degeneracy.
- **`length_chase.dart`** — the step reads. The open spelling question is settled as **products, not ratios**: a chase line is an arbitrary ℚ-combination and a row with three positives and one negative has no canonical ratio split, so ratio-when-possible would be two notations in one chase. Hence no fraction slash. `ProofStep.chase` became the `ArithmeticChase` **interface** — not a base class, since the two chases share a shape and no arithmetic.

**The measurement, run against the same corpus with the publisher off.** One fact of delta in the whole seven fixtures: `provoleas2.json` 118 → 119 facts, `cong` 10 → 11, applications identical (13 268), the other six byte-identical by kind. Publisher cost per pass 10–384 µs — three orders off the 1.3 s a quartic enumeration would have cost. **One fact is the phase's honest yield and not a disappointment**: the closure exists to reach a `cong` the rule table cannot.

**What it proves, end to end.** `provoleas2`'s `|AB| = |LO|`, `verify()` empty, and the chase reads:

```
|OA|^2 = |OB|^2        [1]
|OB| = |ON|            [2]
|MO| = |MA|            [3]
|ON|·|OB| = |MO|·|LO|  [14]
|MA|·|AB| = |OA|^2     [21]
⟹ |AB| = |LO|
```

The squares are the coefficient 2 no union-find over congruence classes can form. Asked through the *Equal lengths* template — four taps into two two-point segment slots — the provider answers **proved**, where before the phase it answered *unproved*.

Gates: analyze clean, suite **3342** green (3275 at the phase's start), browser gate **15** (was 13).

**Next.** Phase 153 (auxiliary construction) is now the large open engine item and the one PLAN says the corpus is waiting on; Phase 161's decision box is the small one. Carried: `readFact` on listed `eqangle`s can still read magnitude-false; Phase 157's and the builder's browser looks unverified.

**Gotchas.**

- **The browser gate caught a guessed expectation**, which is the gate doing its job for a reason that is not compilation: my first web pin asserted the *other* sound reading of one chase line (the elimination doubles the `cong` row rather than halving the `eqratio` row). VM and target agree; both now pin the row the elimination actually chose. Measure a rendering before pinning it on two platforms.
- **A full-suite run took 13 minutes once and was pure machine contention** — `tracing_properties_test.dart` stalled, and it runs in 13 s alone. Later runs were 43 s and 69 s. Before hunting a performance regression in a suite that just got slower, re-run it.
- `ProofStep.chase` is `ArithmeticChase` now, so `.lines` needs a cast. `angle_chase_test` does two, and each is the claim that rig is making rather than a workaround.
- `Fact` canonicalizes its arguments, so which side of a rendered chase line a factor lands on follows the *stored* order, not the order the test wrote them. Three expectations had to be corrected to what comes out; that is a property worth knowing before writing rendering pins.
- `length_closure_upside_test.dart` measured six fixtures and the corpus is seven — `tangent-chord.rgl` was missing for no recorded reason. Fixed here; worth checking the other corpus rigs against `benchmark/prover_chunk_bench.dart`'s list.

## 2026-08-25 — session 176: Phase 153, the rig before the search

**Done — two commits on `phase-153-auxiliary-construction`, four of the phase's seven boxes.** The measurement A2's checklist asked for ("a search, so it needs a budget and an order, both measured rather than chosen"), taken before the search that would use it — Phases 151b and 152e's shape a fifth time.

- **`lib/domain/prover/auxiliary_points.dart`** — the candidate list, in `domain/` because both the measurement and any future engine read it and writing it twice would let them drift. Three families in the checklist's order, deterministic over the objects' own order, so index *i* names the same point in every copy of a document. **The dedup is numeric and that is the decision**: a document already naming the midpoint of `AB` is a structural coincidence, but the foot of a centre on a chord being that chord's midpoint is a *theorem*, and no structural test sees the second kind. That is the `DiagramFilter` boundary, not a breach of it — proposing is a search heuristic, and nothing there asserts a predicate. A structural incidence skip was written first and **deleted**: mutation-checking showed it failed no test the dedup was not already failing.
- **The result: 2 unlocks of 1 fact from 276 candidates over seven documents.** Both on `perp-true-unproved.rgl`, both midpoints, both reaching `perp(C,D,D,F)` — the midpoint of `BC`, which is the point JGEX's dialog names, and the midpoint of `BF` on the same line. The proof verifies and cites the invented point. So A2 is exactly the mechanism that document needs and **nothing else in the corpus needs it**, which is Phase 152e's corpus finding again rather than an exception to it.
- **The metric correction is most of the answer.** A first pass compared fact sets and reported unlocks on six of seven fixtures — every one a re-spelling. Adding *any* point to `no-locus.rgl` "unlocks" `para(F,G,F,B)`, which is the hypothesis `coll(F,G,B)` said in `para` language: the angle closure entails it and declines to *publish* it, a `para` sharing a point being a degeneracy. The screen is `Prover.resolve`, the question a user's *Ask* already goes through. Pinned as its own test, because reading the fact set would have made every number in the phase wrong by an order of magnitude.
- **Order settled, budget opened.** The 87 feet and 45 intersections unlock nothing anywhere, so midpoints-first is a measurement now. And a candidate costs a *full re-run*, so an exhaustive pass is (candidates) × (baseline): 78 × 643 ms = **50 s on `provoleas2.json`** against its own 368 ms, worst single candidate **4.4 s**. The probe is 2 ms of that 50 s; the exchange is the cost. No ordering makes an exhaustive sweep cheap — what an ordering buys is finding the point *early*, which is the claim a search has to be built on.
- **Cost split by document, deliberately.** `provoleas2` and `tangent-chase` are 67 of the sweep's 74 seconds, so the exhaustive form and the timings live in `benchmark/auxiliary_search_bench.dart` (timings not being a thing to assert) and the suite carries the other five in 12 s. Their negative was measured at a 200 000 cap with every candidate run reaching quiescence.

Gates: analyze clean, suite **3352** green (3342 at the phase's start), 50 s wall clock.

**Next.** The remaining three boxes: the search itself (goal-directed, early-exit, bounded in `runChunked`'s currency rather than a fact count), the modal that names an invented point and offers to add it, and a replacement *unproved* rig. Carried: `readFact` on listed `eqangle`s can still read magnitude-false; Phase 157's and the builder's browser looks unverified; Phase 161's decision box.

**Gotchas.**

- **`Set == Set` is identity in Dart**, and it cost a debugging round in a test that looked obviously right. Compare sorted lists, or joined keys.
- **A "new fact" is not new content**, and the whole first pass of this measurement was wrong for that reason. Anything comparing two prover runs should ask the *engine* whether the difference is entailed, not diff the fact set — the closures deliberately withhold degenerate spellings, so the fact set understates what a run knows.
- **`o.circle != null` and `o.conic != null` are different filters** and the candidate count moves by two on `provoleas2` between them; the projective accessor is the one domain code reads, per CLAUDE.md, and it is the one in `lib/`. The scratch rig used the affine getter and under-counted.
- The sweep is **one point at a time**. A theorem needing two auxiliary points comes back as a zero here, and JGEX constructs more than one on harder documents — stated in the test's header so the negative is not read as broader than it is.
